का.आ. 2250.--केन्द्र सरकार, राजभाषा (संघ के शासकीय प्रयोजनों के लिए प्रयोग) नियम,1976 के नियम 10 
के उप-नियम (4) के अनुसरण में राजस्व विभाग के केन्द्रीय अप्रत्यक्ष कर एवं सीमा शुल्क बोर्ड के अधीन केन्द्रीय माल एवं 
सेवा कर (अपील) आयुक्‍क्तालय, गुरुग्राम के कार्यालय, जिसके 80 प्रतिशत से अधिक अधिकारियों एवं कर्मचारियों ने हिन्दी 
का कार्यसाधक ज्ञान प्राप्त कर लिया है, को अधिसूचित करती है।

का.आ. 2253.---सूचना प्रौद्योगिकी (मध्यस्थ और डिजिटल मीडिया आचार संहिता के लिए दिशानिर्देश) 
नियम, 2021 के नियम 3 के उप-नियम (1) के खंड (घ) के साथ पठित सूचना प्रौद्योगिकी अधिनियम, 2000 (2000 का 
21) की धारा 79 की उप-धारा (3) के खंड (A) के अनुसरण में, केंद्र सरकार एतद्द्वारा, विदेशी रोजगार में संबंधित 
मामलों पर गैरकानूनी जानकारी के संबंध में मध्यस्थों को टेक डाउन नोटिस जारी करने के लिए विदेश मंत्रालय में संयुक्त 
सचिव (प्रवासी रोजगार) एवं उत्प्रवासी महासंरक्षक को नोडल अधिकारी के रूप में नामित करती है। 


[फा. सं. Z-385/05/2025-LP&G]

(Overseas Employment and Protector General of Emigrants Division) 
New Delhi, the 17th December, 2025

का.आ. 2255.--केंद्रीय सरकार, भारतीय नागरिक सुरक्षा संहिता, 2023 (2023 का 46) की धारा 18 की 
उपधारा (8) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए, श्री कश्मीर सिंह ठाकुर, अधिवक्ता के नाम को, हिमाचल प्रदेश उच्च 
न्यायालय, शिमला के समक्ष विशेष लोक अभियोजक के रूप में, केंद्रीय अन्वेषण ब्यूरो द्वारा दिल्‍ली विशेष पुलिस स्थापन, 
(केन्द्रीय अन्वेषण ब्यूरो) द्वारा संस्थित मामलों से Saye अभियोजन, अपील, पुनरीक्षण और अन्य मामलों का संचालन 
करने से हटाती है और इस प्रयोजन के लिए भारत सरकार के कार्मिक, लोक शिकायत और पेंशन मंत्रालय की अधिसूचना 
संख्यांक का.आ.2079 तारीख 23 सितंबर, 2024 में निम्नलिखित संशोधन करती है, अर्थात्‌ः- 


उक्त अधिसूचना में, “अधिवक्तागण श्री कश्मीर सिंह ठाकुर और श्री जनेश महाजन” शब्दों के स्थान पर “श्री जनेश 
महाजन अधिवक्ता” शब्द रखे जाएंगे। 


[फा. सं. 225/22/2024-एवीडी-1] 


सत्यम श्रीवास्तव, अवर सचिव 


New Delhi, the 26th December, 2025 


S.O. 2255.—In exercise of the powers conferred by sub-section (8) of section 18 of the Bharatiya Nagarik 
Suraksha Sanhita, 2023 (46 of 2023), the Central Government hereby removes the name of Shri Kashmir Singh 
Thakur, as Special Public Prosecutor for conducting the prosecution, appeals, revisions and other matters arising out 
of the cases instituted by the Delhi Special Police Establishment (Central Bureau of Investigation) before the High 
Court of Himachal Pradesh at Shimla and for that purpose, hereby makes the following amendment in the 
notification of the Government of India, in the Ministry of Public Grievances and Pensions number S.O. 2079, 
dated the 23rd September, 2024, namely:— 


In the said notification, for the words “Shri Kashmir Singh Thakur and Shri Janesh Mahajan, Advocates”, 
the words “Shri Janesh Mahajan, Advocate” shall be substituted. 


[F. No. 225/22/2024-AVD-I] 
SATYAM SRIVASTAVA, Under Secy. 


नई दिल्‍ली, 26 दिसम्बर, 2025

अधिनियम XXV) की धारा 5 की उप-धारा (1) सपठित धारा 6 द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए पुलिस उप 
महानिरीक्षक/शाखाप्रमुख, सीबीआई, एसीबी, रांची के दिनांक 17.10.2025 के पत्र संख्या 929/2025-आर द्वारा किए 
गए अनुरोध पर झारखंड राज्य सरकार की अधिसूचना ज्ञापन सं.-10/सी.बी.आई.-428/2025-6765, रांची, दिनांक 
11.11.2025, गृह, कारागार और आपदा प्रबंधन विभाग के माध्यम से जारी सम्मति से श्री रंजन, डाक सहायक, डाक 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जनवरी 10, 2026/पौष 20, 1947 7727 


विभाग, संचार मंत्रालय एवं अन्य अज्ञात सरकारी सेवक(कों) के विरुद्ध भ्रष्टाचार निवारण अधिनियम, 1988 (2018 में 
यथा संशोधित) की धारा 7 के तहत अपराध करने के लिए दिनांक 11.11.2025 को पंजीकृत आरसी 0242025ए0015 
में साथ ही इस मामले के अन्वेषण के दौरान प्रकाश में आए किन्ही अन्य अपराधों एवं ऐसे एक या उससे अधिक अपराधों 
से जुड़े या उससे संबद्ध किसी दुष्प्रयास, दुष्प्रेरणा और षड्यंत्र एवं/अथवा उसी संव्यवहार में किए गए या उन्हीं तथ्यों से 
उत्पन्न किसी अन्य अपराध(धों) के लिए मामला दर्ज करने एवं अन्वेषण करने के लिए दिल्ली विशेष पुलिस स्थापन के 
सदस्यों की शक्तियों और क्षेत्राधिकार का विस्तार (कार्योत्तर प्रभाव से, दिनांक 11.11.2025 से) झारखंड राज्य में 
करती है।

facto w.e.f. 11.11.2025) for registration and investigation in RCO242025A0015 registered on 11.11.2025 against

for committing offence u/s 7 of PC Act-1988 (as amended in 2018) and any other offences that may come to light

का.आ. 2257.--केंद्रीय सरकार, दिल्‍ली विशेष पुलिस स्थापन अधिनियम, 1946 (1946 का केंद्रीय 
अधिनियम 25) की धारा 5 की उप-धारा (1) सपठित धारा 6 द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए अधिसूचना सं. 
जी.ओ.(2डी) .सं. 351, दिनांक 31.10.2025, गृह (पुलिस-2(५) विभाग के माध्यम से जारी तमिलनाडु राज्य सरकार की 
सम्मति से श्री एस.के.एस.नागराजन, श्री अरियामुथु गुनासेकर, श्री मारन रबीन्द्र सैलापति, श्री अरियामुथु वीरमनी और 
मेसर्स फेंस एजुकेशन अकेडमी प्राइवेट लिमिटेड, तिरुनेलवेलि के विरुद्ध भारतीय न्याय संहिता, 2023 (2023 का केंद्रीय 
अधिनियम 45) की धारा 61(1) सपठित भ्रष्टाचार निवारण अधिनियम, 1988 (1988 का केंद्रीय अधिनियम 49) (वर्ष 
2018 में यथासंशोधित) की धारा 7 और 11 के तहत मामले के पंजीकरण हेतु दिल्‍ली विशेष पुलिस स्थापन के सदस्यों की 
शक्तियों और क्षेत्राधिकार का विस्तार समस्त तमिलनाडु राज्य में करती है।

[भाग [ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जनवरी 10, 2026/पौष 20, 1947 7731 


AWARD 


In exercise of powers conferred under clause (d) of sub-section (1) and sub —section (2A) of Section 10 of 
the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947) Government Of India through the Ministry of Labour, vide its Order 
No.L-20012/3 1/2008- IR(CM-I) dated 28/04/2008 has been pleased to refer the following dispute between the 
employer i.e. management of Lodna Area of M/s. BCCL, Dhanbad and their workman through Joint General 
Secretary, Bahujan Mazdoor Union, Dhansar, Dhanbad for adjudication by this Tribunal: 


SCHEDULE 


“Whether the action of the management of Bagdigi Colliery of M/s. BCCL in not providing 
dependent employment to Sh. Bhola Bhuia, S/o- Late Ganauri Bhuia, Loader, under the provisions 
of NCWA is justified and legal? If not, to what relief is the dependent son of Late Ganauri Bhuia 
entitled?” 


2 It is relevant to mention here that this reference case was already disposed of vide this court award dated 
19.11.15 and thereafter when the same was challenged by the management before the Hon’ble High Court of 
Jharkhand at Ranchi, the Hon’ble Court was pleased to set aside the Award dated 19.11.2015 passed by this Court and 
remitted back this reference case to decide the dispute and pass final order after hearing both the parties. 


3. The case of the applicant as per the written statement filed on behalf of the union on 24.09.2008 is that 
Ganauri Bhuia designated as Ex-Loader bearing his CMPF A/c No. C/17/327 and I.D. Card No. 154788 was a 
permanent workman in Bagdigi Colliery under Lodna Area of M/s. BCCL who died on 13.03.1994 while in service. 
At the time of death his dependent son Bhola Bhuia was teenage and a representation was submitted for employment 
to the dependent. When Bhola Bhuia reached the age of 18 years, he approached to the authority concerned to get 
employment in place of his deceased father in accordance with the provisions of NCWA-IV but it was not considered. 
Sri Bhola Bhuia dependent of Late Ganauri Bhuia is entitled to get employment in place of his deceased father as per 
the above provision and the action of the management of Bagdigi Colliery of M/s. BCCL in not providing 
employment to Sri Bhola Bhuia is not justified. The demand of the employment by Bhola Bhuia is legal and justified 
and it was prayed to pass award in favour of the applicant. 


4. On the other hand the case of the management as per written statement cum rejoinder dated 16.07.2019 is 
that the present reference is bad in law and is liable to be dismissed. The claim of the dependent is hopelessly barred 
by limitation. The dependent claimant had not come up before this Tribunal with clean hands. It is fact that Late 
Ganauri Bhuia was working as Loader at Bagdigi Colliery at the time of his death and as per official record his year 
of birth was 1956 and he was appointed on 17.04.1978 and he expired on 13.03.1994. In the service excerpt of Late 
Ganauri Bhuia the name of Budhni Bhuini appears as wife and Bhola Bhuia, the claimant appears as son. It has 
further been submitted that Budhni Bhuini during her life time had declared herself as widow of Dwarika Bhuia and 
in the service excerpt of Late Budhni Bhuini, Dwarika Bhuia appears as father but in other records her husband’s 
name is mentioned as Late Dwarika Bhuia. Bhola Bhuia has filed an affidavit wherein he has mentioned his father’s 
name as Late Dwarika Bhuia while submitting application for employment in place of her mother Late Budhni 
Bhuini. Therefore, it is crystal clear that the claimant Bhola Bhuia is not the son of Late Ganauri Bhuia. Before the 
Conciliation Officer also he could not produce any chit of paper to prove that he is the son of Late Ganauri Bhuia 
while claiming employment in his place. The claim of employment has been filed at a very belated stage and it is well 
covered by the judgments of the Hon’ble Court that compassionate appointment cannot be claimed as a matter of 
right and it cannot be kept reserve for long and the management had rightly denied the claim of the claimant. In his 
rejoinder he has submitted that the statement made in Para-1 of ws by the union requires no comment. The statement 
made in Para-2 by the union is matter of record. The Statement made in Para-3 by the union is denied as Bhola Bhuia 
has filed affidavit declaring himself as son of Late Dwarika Bhuia and he has never submitted any representation after 
the death of Ganauri Bhuia before the management for employment. The statement made in Para-4, 5, 6 and 7 by the 
union is denied as the case of Bhola Bhuia does not fall within the purview of NCWA and he has himself declared as 
son of Dwarika Bhuia and so the denial of employment is legally justified and the demand of the union is illegal and 
prayed to hold the action of the management in not providing employment to Bhola Bhuia is fair, legal and justified. 


5. Rejoinder has also been filed on behalf of the union submitting therein that the fact as content in Para-1 of 
the W/S of the management is a fact. The Statement made in Para-2, 3, 4, 5, 8, 9, 10, 11 and 12 has been denied as not 
correct. The statements made in Para-6 and 7 is matter of record and the facts mentioned in Para-13 is matter of 
policy. At last it was prayed to pass Award in favour of the claimant. 


6. Issue 


Whether the action of the management of Bagdigi Colliery of M/s. BCCL in not providing dependent 
employment to Sh. Bhola Bhuia, S/o- Late Ganauri Bhuia, Loader, under the provisions of NCWA is justified and 
legal? If not, to what relief is the dependent son of Late Ganauri Bhuia entitled?” 


7. In support of his case the union has examined WW-1, Bhola Bhuiya. 


77132 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JANUARY 10, 2026/PAUSHA 20, 1947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


8. In support of his case the union has adduced the following documentary evidence: - 
Exhibit W-1 - Death Certificate of Late Ganauri Bhuia. 

Exhibit W-2 — Service Excerpts of Late Ganauri Bhuia. 

9. In support of its case the management has examined MW-1, Prabhat Kumar. 

10. The management has adduced the following documentary evidence in support of its case:- 
Exhibit M-1 - Copy of Circular of BCCL dated 06/24.01.2004. 

Exhibit M-2 — Copy of Service Excerpt of Late Ganauri Bhuia. 

Exhibit M-3 - Copy of Service Excerpt of Late Budhni Bhuini. 

Exhibit M-4 — Letter dated 12/14.04.1994 to the Agent, Bagdigi Colliery Lodna Area - X. 


Exhibit M-5 —Letter of Project Officer/Manager, Bagdigi Colliery to the Assistant Labour Commissioner (Central) 
Dhanbad. 


Exhibit M-6 Series — Letter to Secretary, Govt. of India, Ministry of Labour, New Delhi written by Assistant Labour 
Commissioner (Central), Dhanbad-III regarding failure of conciliation report. 


11. Heard both sides and perused the materials on record. 


12. The learned Representative of the claimant has submitted that this is a case of dependent employment and 
Ganauri Bhuia was a permanent workman at Bagdigi Colliery under M/s. BCCL who died in harness on 13.03.1994 
and at the time of death, the claimant Bhola Bhuia who was dependent son of the said deceased, was teenage. So the 
wife of the said deceased workman made a representation before the concerned management to record the name of his 
son Sri Bhola Bhuia in live Roster but management did not accept her representation. When Bhola Bhuia reached the 
age of 18 years, he represented himself before the authority concerned for providing him employment in place of his 
deceased father in accordance with the provisions of NCWA-IV but the concerned authority did not provide him 
employment. He has also submitted death certificate of his late father as well as his service excerpts in which the 
name of Bhola Bhuia has been recorded as the son of the deceased but ultimately his case was rejected by the 
management whereas he is legally entitled for dependent employment on compassionate ground and prayed to pass 
award in his favour. 


13. On the other hand the Ld. Counsel for the management has submitted that Ganauri Bhuia who was Ex- 
employee of Bagdigi Colliery died on 13.03.1994 and in his service excerpts the name of Budhni Bhuini as wife and 
Bhola Bhuia as son has been recorded but Budhni Bhuini during her life time declared herself as widow of Dwarika 
Bhuia but in her service excerpts the name of Dwarika Bhuia appears as father. The claimant Bhola Bhuia has also 
filed an affidavit before the management wherein he has mentioned his father’s name as Dwarika Bhuiya while 
submitting application for employment in place of his mother Budhni Bhuini which shows that Bhola Bhuia is not the 
son of Late Ganauri Bhuia and so he is not entitled to get employment in his place. He also could not produce any 
document before Conciliation Officer to prove his father’s name as Ganauri Bhuia. The present case has been filed 
after about 10 years of the death of Ganauri Bhuia and so it is hopelessly by barred by limitation. The compassionate 
appointment is not a matter of right. At the time of death of Ganauri Bhuia NCWA-V was in operation and effective 
since 01.07.1991 to 30.06.1996 and as per the same it is clear that a male dependent of the concerned worker if of 15 
years or above in age at the time of death, he will be kept on live Roster and would be provided employment 
commensurate with his skill and qualification when he attains the age of 18 years whereas the claimant Bhola Bhuia 
was only 9 years old at the time of death and so the claim is time barred and he is not entitled for any compassionate 
appointment. 


14. As regards the oral evidence of the union, WW-1 Bhola Bhuia, claimant himself has deposed that he is the 
son of Late Ganauri Bhuia who was permanent workman in Bagdigi Colliery of M/s. BCCL and died on 13.03.1994 
while in service. His name also appears in his father’s service excerpts showing his relationship as son. At the time of 
death of his father he was teenager and after reaching 18 years he applied for his employment which was regretted by 
the higher authority. He is entitled to get employment in place of his deceased father in accordance with the provision 
of NCWA-IV. In his cross-examination he has stated that his father died on 13.03.1994. He was working as loader in 
Bagdigi Colliery but his mother Budhni Devi was not a working leady. She died two to three years back but he can 
not tell the year. He has denied the fact that his mother was working in BCCL. He has denied of filing any affidavit 
before BCCL showing his father’s name as Dwarika Bhuia. 


15. As regards the documentary evidence of the claimant, Exhibit W-1 is the photo copy of Death Certificate of 
Ganuari Bhuia and on its perusal it transpires that his date of death has been mentioned as 13.03.1994. Exhibit W-2 is 
the photo copy of Service Excerpts of Late Ganauri Bhuia and on its perusal it transpires that the name of Budhni 
Bhuini has been mentioned as wife and the name of claimant Bhola Bhuia has been mentioned as son in the column 
of relationship. 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जनवरी 10, 2026/पौष 20, 1947 7733 


16. As regards the oral evidence of the management, MW-1, Prabhat Kumar has deposed that claimant has 
sought for job in place of his deceased father who died on 13.03.1994. As per service excerpts the age of the claimant 
was 9 years at the time of his father death. He was not entitled to be enrolled in service excerpts. In the cross- 
examination he has admitted that the claimant’s name is there in his father service excerpts and he has denied that the 
claimant is entitled to get job on compassionate ground. 


17. As regards the documentary evidence of the Management, Exhibit M-1 is the Circular dated 6/24.01.2004 of 
the BCCL and according to this circular, no petition for dependent employment will be entertained after 18 months 
from the date of death or disablement. Exhibit M-2 is the photo copy of Service Excerpts of Late Ganauri Bhuia and 
on its perusal it transpires that the name of Budhni Bhuini has been mentioned as wife and the name of claimant 
Bhola Bhuia has been mentioned as son in the column of relationship. Exhibit M-3 is the photo copy of service 
excerpts of Budhni Bhuini and on its perusal it transpires that the name of Bhola Bhuia has been mentioned in the 
column of nominee as well as in the column of her son. Exhibit M-4 is related to the nominee of Ganauri Bhuia 
regarding his P.F in which the name of his mother has been mentioned which is not relevant for this instant issue. 
Exhibit M-5 is the letter of Project Officer/Manager of Bagdigi Colliery written to the Assistant Labour 
Commissioner (Central), Dhanbad regarding employment to Sri Bhola Bhuia and on its perusal it transpires that it has 
been mentioned therein that Ganauri Bhuia was Ex-employee of Bagdigi Colliery and he died on 13.03.1994 and at 
that time the age of Bhola Bhuia was 9 years and so at that stage the employment could not be given to the dependent 
of Ganauri Bhuia being belated case and it was prayed to close the conciliation. Exhibit M-6 is the letter dated 
25.08.2003 written by Assistant Labour Commissioner (Central), Dhanbad to the Secretary Government of India of 
Ministry of Labour, New Delhi regarding information on failure of the conciliation report. 


18. On perusal of the oral as well as documentary evidence of the claimant, Bhola Bhuia as well as the 
management (Exhibit W-2, M-2) it is established that the claimant, Bhola Bhuia was the son of Late Ganauri Bhuia, 
Ex-employee of BCCL and there was relationship of father and son between them and the oral and empty claim of the 
management that the claimant, Bhola Bhuia has filed an affidavit for employment mentioning the name of his father 
as Dwarika Bhuia has not been supported by any documentary evidence and therefore this claim is out rightly brushed 
aside. Secondly the oral and empty claim of the management that Budhni Bhuini has declared herself to be wife of 
Dwarika Bhuia in other documents is also not supported by any documentary evidence before this Tribunal and 
therefore this claim is also out rightly brushed aside. 


19. Now as regard the entitlement of the claimant, Bhola Bhuia for dependent job in place of his father Late 
Ganauri Bhuia after his death on 13.03.1994, the claimant has simply claimed that at the time of death of his father 
Ganauri Bhuia he was teenager and after reaching the age of 18 years he had applied for employment which was 
regretted by the higher authority of the BCCL but he has nowhere disclosed either in pleading or in evidence as to 
what was his actual age or date of birth at the time of death of his father and on which date he attained the age of 
majority ie 18 years and on which date thereafter he filed his representation before the BCCL for dependent 
employment and whatever he has mentioned in his pleading and evidence on this crucial point, that is all cryptic and 
vague. Not only this, he has not also taken any pain to file a copy of representation or any date of birth certificate, 
Aadhar Card etc from his side. 


On the other hand, the management has regretted the claim of dependent employment of the claimant Bhola 
Bhuia stating that at the time of death of Ganauri Bhuia NCWA-V was applicable (agreement covered period from 1 
July, 1991 to June 30,1996) and as per the same in case of death either in mine accident or for other reasons or medical 
unfitness under clause 9.4.0, if no employment has been offered and the male dependent of the concerned worker is 
15 years and above in age, he will be kept on a live Roster and would be provided employment commensurate with 
his skill and qualification when attains the age of 18 years. Here in the instant case though the claimant has not stated 
anything and kept mum on the point as to what was the actual age of the claimant at the time of death of his father 
Ganauri Bhuia but on perusal of Ext M-5 which has been submitted from the side of the management, it transpires 
that at the time of death of the Ex-employee Ganauri Bhuia the age of Bhola Bhuia was 9 years. Though on perusal of 
Exhibit M-2 and Exhibit W-2 it appears that at the time of preparation of the service excerpts the age of the claimant, 
Bhola Bhuia was 2 years but these documents are so illegible that it is very difficult to scan the date of preparation of 
these documents. 


20. On the basis of above discussions this Tribunal finds that at the time of death of Ganauri Bhuia, the age of 
Bhola Bhuia was only 9 years and so he was not put on live Roster as per the provisions of NCWA V and therefore 
there is no question of consideration of his employment on compassionate ground and secondly, admittedly after 
attaining majority, the claimant Bhola Bhuia filed his claim for dependent employment (though no copy of the claim 
application has been filed) which was highly time barred and on that ground his claim was rightly regretted by the 
BCCL because this is settled principle of law that the compassionate appointment is given to provide immediate 
financial assistance to a family facing sudden crisis and financial destitution due to the death or medical invalidation 
of the primary bread winner whereas Exhibit M-3 proves that the mother of the claimant Budhni Bhuini was also 
BCCL employee and she was appointed on 23.11.1981 about 13 years prior to the death of Ganauri Bhuia and 
therefore there is no question of any financial destitution of family due to the death of Ganauri Bhuia and for these 
reasons the claim of the claimant, Bhola Bhuia for compassionate appointment is not at all sustainable in the 
eye of law. 


77134 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JANUARY 10, 2026/PAUSHA 20, 1947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


21. Thus, on the basis of above appreciations of the facts, circumstances and evidence on record and the reasons 
stated above this Tribunal finds that the claim of the claimant Bhola Bhuia for compassionate appointment is not 
sustainable in the eye of law. Accordingly, it is held that the action of the management of Bagdigi Colliery of M/s. 
BCCL in not providing dependent employment to Sri Bhola Bhuia, S/o- Late Ganauri Bhuia, Loader under the 
provisions of NCWA is quite justified and legal and he is not entitled to any relief. The reference is answered 
accordingly. 


Let the copies of Award in duplicate be sent to the Ministry of Labour& Employment, Government of 
India, New Delhi for information and notification. 


SACHINDRA KUMAR PANDEY, Presiding Officer 
नई दिल्‍ली, 29 दिसम्बर, 2025

सरकार एयरपोर्ट अथॉरिटी ऑफ इंडिया; मेसर्स ओमेगा एंटरप्राइजेज; मेसर्स ओलंपिया टेक पार्क (चेन्नई) प्राइवेट 
लिमिटेड; मेसर्स मीनांबक्कम रियल्टी प्राइवेट लिमिटेड के प्रबंधतंत्र के संबद्ध नियोजकों और चेन्नई एयरपोर्ट कॉन्ट्रैक्ट वर्कर्स 
यूनियन के बीच अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट केन्द्रीय सरकार औद्योगिक अधिकरण एवं श्रम न्यायालय, चेन्नई, पंचाट (रिफरेन्स 
न.- 54/2023) को जैसा कि अनुलग्नक में दिखाया गया है, प्रकाशित करती है जो केन्द्रीय सरकार को सॉफ्ट कॉपी के साथ 
30.12.2025 को प्राप्त हुआ था |

Government hereby publishes the award (Reference No. 54/2023) of the Central Government Industrial Tribunal 
cum Labour Court, Chennai as shown in the Annexure, in the Industrial dispute between the employers in relation 
to Airport Authority of India; M/s Omega Enterprises; M/s Olympia Tech Park (Chennai) Pvt. Ltd.;

AWARD 


The Central Government, Ministry of Labour & Employment vide its Order No. L-11011/03/2022-IR(M), 
dated 17.07.2023 referred the following Industrial Dispute to this Tribunal for adjudication. 


The schedule mentioned in that order is: 


“1. Whether the dispute raised by Chennai Airport Contract Worker’s Union vide their representation dated 
20.06.2022 1.7.0. 123 workers against the management of Airports Authority of India, Chennai / M/s Omega 
Enterprises, Mumbai / Olympia Tech Park Private Limited, Chennai is industrial dispute under ID Act, 1947? 


2. If yes at S.No.1 above, whether the claim of Chennai Airport Contract Worker’s Union vide their representation 
dated 20.06.2022 that the contractual arrangement between the contractor and the management of Airports Authority 
of India, Chennai for engagement of 123 workers (list enclosed) is sham and nominal is proper, legal and justified? If 
yes, to what reliefs as sought vide letter under reference the disputant union/workers are entitled and what directions, 
if any are necessary in the matter?” 


2; On receipt of the above reference dated 31.10.2023 from the appropriate Government, the dispute was 
registered as ID No. 54/2023 and due notices were issued to both the parties for their appearance fixing the case to 
31.10.2023. Neither the Petitioner nor any Counsel / Authorized Representative turned up resulting further 
adjournments to 02.01.2024, 05.08.2025 and 03.10.2025. The Petitioner did not turn up whereas the First and Second 
Respondents files their Vakalat Nama. It reveals from the body of reference that while the appropriate Govt. sent the 
reference dated 17.07.2023 to this Tribunal for adjudication, copies of the reference were sent to the Petitioner and all 
the opposite parties. It is therefore well presumed that both parties must have received the reference. Even then, in 
the interest of justice, the Petitioner was once again directed to appear and to file claim statement fixing the case to 
18.11.2025. The Petitioner did not turn up nor was any authorized representative of the counsel on his behalf present. 
The claim statement was not furnished in any manner by the Petitioner. The R3 and R4 were not present, whereas R1 
and R2 were present. It reveals the Petitioner, despite of sufficient opportunity choose not to appear or to file claim 
statement and documents. 


3: In the circumstance it deems proper not to re-list the case for the same purpose to any other date which 
would be wastage of the valuable time of this Tribunal. The case was reserved for Final Order. 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जनवरी 10, 2026/पौष 20, 1947 7737 


4. In view of the discussion held supra, it is crystal clear that the petitioner has got no interest to proceed with 
the case. Thus, the Tribunal is not in a position to adjudicate the dispute as referred by the Appropriate Government, 
as there exists no Industrial Dispute for adjudication as per the reference. 


5. In the result the reference is answered against the petitioner. 
An Award is passed accordingly. 
SUSHIL KUMAR-I, Presiding Officer

का.आ. 2264.--औद्योगिक विवाद अधिनियम, 1947 (1947 का 14) की धारा 17 के अनुसरण में, केन्द्रीय 
सरकार मेसर्स भारत पेट्रोलियम कॉर्पोरेशन लिमिटेड के प्रबंधतंत्र के संबद्ध नियोजकों और उनके कर्मचारी के बीच अनुबंध 
में निर्दिष्ट केन्द्रीय सरकार औद्योगिक अधिकरण एवं श्रम न्‍न्यायालय-1, धनबाद, पंचाट (रिफरेन्स न.- 44/2007) को जैसा 


कि अनुलग्रक में दिखाया गया है, प्रकाशित करती है जो केन्द्रीय सरकार को सॉफ्ट कॉपी के साथ 30.12.2025 को प्राप्त 
हुआ था | 


[सं. एल-30012/10/2007-आईआर/(एम)] 


सलोनी, उप निदेशक 


New Delhi, the 30th December, 2025 


9.0. 2264.—In pursuance of Section 17 of the Industrial Dispute Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central 
Government hereby publishes the award (Reference No. 44/2007) of the Central Government Industrial Tribunal 
cum Labour Court-1, Dhanbad as shown in the Annexure, in the Industrial dispute between the employers in 
relation to M/s Bharat Petroleum Corporation Ltd. and Their Workmen which was received along with soft copy 
of the award by the Central Government on 30.12.2025. 


[No. L-30012/10/2007— IR (M)] 
SALONI, Dy. Director 
ANNEXURE 
BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL NO.1, DHANBAD 
In the matter of reference U/S 10 (1) (9) & (2A) of I.D.Act. 1947 
Reference Case No. 44/2007 


Parties: 
Employers in relation to the management of M/s. Bharat Petroleum 
Corporation Ltd., Dhanbad 
AND 
Their Workmen 
Present: Shri Sachindra Kumar Pandey 
Presiding Officer 
Appearances: 
For the Employers :-Sri V.K. Pandey, Ld. Advocate 
For the workmen :- Sri Pintu Mondal, Ld. Representative 
State : Jharkhand. Industry:-Petroluem 
Dated 02/12/2025 
AWARD 


In exercise of powers conferred under clause (d) of sub-section (1) and sub —section (2A) of Section 10 of 
the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947) Government Of India through the Ministry of Labour, vide its Order 
No.L-30012/10/2007 (IR(M)) dated 27/08/2007 has been pleased to refer the following dispute between the 
employer i.e. management of M/s. BPCL, Dhanbad and their workman through Secretary, Jharkhand Janta 
Mazdoor Union, Saraidhela, Dhanbad for adjudication by this Tribunal: 


77138 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JANUARY 10, 2026/PAUSHA 20, 1947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


“Whether the action of the management of BPCL, Dhanbad Depot in terminating the services of 
Sri GholtuTuri, who worked (as Heavy Vehicle Driver) from October, 1989 to 18/10/2002 without 
following the provisions of ID Act, 1947 is just, legal and fair? If not, what relief the workman is 
entitled to?” 


2. The case of the workman as per the written statement filed on 06.04.2011 is that the workman 
concerned was a permanent employee of BPCL, Dhanbad Depot and he was appointed in the year 1989 and he 
rendered continuous service with unblemished record till 18.10.2002 when the management terminated his service in 
utter violation of principles of natural justice without assigning any reason. In the year 1989 the name of the workman 
concerned was sponsored by the Employment Exchange of Government of Bihar and he was invited by the 
management of BPCL to appear in the written test cum interview for Heavy Vehicle Driver. Accordingly he appeared 
and after written test he was declared successful and then invited for personal interview and after the interview he was 
declared successful and the management appointed him and since then he was working as Heavy Vehicle Driver till 
the management terminated his services illegally. He was paid wages by the management on monthly rate prescribed 
by the management from time to time. He was paid ex-gratia payment according to the rules of the management. He 
worked continuously for 13 years but even then the management terminated his services without following the 
mandatory provisions of Certified Standing Orders of the company as well as the mandatory provisions of the I.D. 
Act, 1947. Just to deprive him from legitimate right and benefits the management made some paper arrangements and 
showing him as casual employees which is a camouflage to conceal the real issue. Although he was reporting for his 
duties regularly without any break but just to deprive him from legitimate right, the management created some 
artificial break in his record. He has acquired all the status of a permanent workman and his termination from service 
amounts to unfair labour practice. He was not issued any charge sheet or show cause notice before terminating his 
services which is against the principles of natural justice and therefore he is entitled to be reinstated with full back 
wages and other consequential benefits and it was prayed to hold the action of the management of M/s. BPCL, 
Dhanbad Depot in terminating the services of the workman Sri GholtuTuri is not just and also illegal. 


3. On the other hand the case of the management as per the written statement filed on 25.08.2011 is that 
the reference itself is bad in law and not maintainable as GholtuTuri was never a permanent employee of the 
Corporation and so he can not be terminated by the Corporation from his services. As per the common practice 
prevalent throughout all the industries, casual workmen are intermittently engaged to cater to either sudden spurt of 
increased workload or to cater in continuity of work in the event of any planned/unplanned leave of permanent 
workmen. The question is how many days in a year such casual engagement of Sri GholtuTuri was done by the 
Corporation. It is worthwhile to mention that such engagements were much below 240 days in a year and particulars 
of the same are as follows:- 


Year Number of Days engaged 
1989-90 67 
1990-91 30 
1991-92 108 
1992-93 155 
1993-94 167.5 
1994-95 149 
1995-96 192.5 
1996-97 184 
1997-98 119 
1998-99 109 
1999-2000 135 
2000-01 128 


From the above, it is evident that Sri GholtuTuri has worked occasionally and intermittently and there was never any 
continuous nature of work for which he was engaged in a year. It would also be clear from the above details that Sri 
Turi never worked for 240 days or more in a year. The workman’s engagement was purely need based and procedure 
followed towards such engagement was as follows:- 


[भाग --खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जनवरी 10, 2026/पौष 20, 1947 7739 


That every temporary need for casual workma was notified with the concerned Employment Exchange to sponsor the 
names of candidates for empanelment as casuals with intimation that such engagement would depend upon casual 
need against absenteeism of permanent workmen only. We also obtained sponsorship from the District Employment 
Exchange with clear message to the Employment Exchange that some of the persons sponsored by them would be 
engaged by us purely as casual workmen. In other words, engagement of any person for work of casual nature does 
not entail any employment offer by the corporation in its permanent cadre and this fact is also very well known to Sri 
GholtuTuri who performed the casual nature of job in the corporation with full knowledge of the status of his 
employment. 


The corporation being declared as a public utility services as per Section 2 (n) of the Industrial Dispute Act 1947 as 
well as amongst the list of industries involving Hazardous Processes as per First Schedule of the Factory Act, 1948 
handling highly inflammable liquids, it is imperative for the Corporation to check the suitability of the candidates 
regarding their fitness for empanelment as casuals to ensure safety of its Depot/Location and the properties contained 
therein. Sri GholtuTuri was empanelled as Casual after carrying out due process of suitability verification, keeping 
safety aspect in mind. 


Sri GhotuTuri was engaged as casual nature of work depending upon the emergent work requirements. He was 
engaged intermittently and was paid in line with the work carried out by him. The casual engagement was 
discontinued in the year 2002 since casual nature of work requirement was tapered off. After October 2002 he was not 
re-engaged since there was no requirement in the establishment. 


In these premises the question of alleged termination of service does not arise since there was no formal letter of 
appointment issued to him as a permanent workman in the Corporation. 


The Constitution Bench judgment of the Hon’ble Supreme Court in case of Secretary, State of Karnataka & OrsVs. 
Uma devi and Ors has strongly turned down such demands by the casuals. Similarly in case of Union of India & 
OrsVs. A.S. Pillai & Ors and State of Rajasthan & Ors. Vs. Daya Lal & Ors, the same thing was held. It has been 
denied that the workman was permanent employee of the Corporation at Dhanbad Depot. It has also been denied that 
he was working as General workman till the management terminated his service. It has also denied that the workman 
acquired all status of a permanent workman. The allegations of termination of service are totally baseless as no 
appointment letter was ever issued to the workman and he was engaged as casual labourer. It is stated that engaging 
casuals as per temporary requirement is prevalent in all industries and the Corporation followed the said process 
subject to its discretion to engage casuals against absenteeism or sudden spurt of work. The question of issuing charge 
sheet by the Corporation does not arise at all as the workman concerned is not permanent employee in the 
Corporation. His reinstatement is also absolutely irrelevant and misconception of law and facts. The Corporation 
denies that the relief sought by the union is legal or justified. It has further denied that the union or the casual 
workman involved in the reference are entitled for any relief and prayed to dismiss the reference. 


4. On perusal of the case record it transpires that the concerned workman died during the pendency of this case 
and in his place his son LakhanTuri has been substituted for the purpose of this case. 


5. A rejoinder has also been filed on behalf of the workman to the W/S filed by the management in which the 
workman has denied almost all stand taken by the management in his W/S stating that the workman has worked 
continuously without any break. 


6. In support of the case of the workman, LakhanTuri has been examined as WW-1. 
7. In his support the workman has adduced the following documentary evidence :- 
Exhibit W-1 — Letter dated 27.07.1989 to the workman GholtuTuri for written test cum interview. 


Exhibit W-2 —Letter dated 18.10.1989 to Sri GholtuTuri from the side of the BPCL to call upon with all the 
documents regarding educational qualification, date of birth etc. for casual labour. 


Exhibit W-3 - A copy of Attendance Sheet of April, 1990. 
Exhibit W-4 - Copy of payment of Ex-Gratia to casual workmen for 2000-2001. 
Exhibit W-5 — Copy of Ex-Gratia payment from 01.04.1992 to 31.03.1993. 


Exhibit W-6 — Letter dated 07.08.1983 of the BPCL, Dhanbad Depot to CPM/Calcutta regarding casual 
employment. 


Exhibit W-7 — Photo copy of Aadhaar Card of LakhanTuri. 
Exhibit W-8 - Photo copy of Electoral 1.10. Card of LakhanTuri. 
Exhibit W-9 — Photo copy of family list certificate. 

Exhibit W-10 - Death Certificate of the workman GholtuTuri. 


77140 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JANUARY 10, 2026/PAUSHA 20, 1947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


8. On the other hand the management has examined MW-1 Sonam Rajgaria. 


9. By way of documentary evidence the management has got exhibited the monthly attendance of the workman 
GholtuTuri and others as M-1 Series. 


10. Heard both sides and perused the materials on record. 


11. The Ld. Representative of the workman has submitted that the workman GholtuTuri was appointed 
by the BPCL in the year 1989 after taking written examination as well as interview but he was illegally terminated on 
18.10.2002 by the management without assigning any reason. He had rendered continuous service with unblemished 
record. He was also paid ex-gratia payment during his service period and thus he had acquired all the status of 
permanent workman but without following the provisions of I.D. Act, 1947 he was illegally terminated by the 
management and prayed to set aside the termination order and pass award for reinstatement of the workman with 
consequential benefits and back wages. 


12. On the other hand the Ld. Counsel on behalf of the management has submitted that the workman 
was appointed by the management purely as casual workman only to cater the increased workload as well as 
continuity in the work in the event of leave of a permanent workman and secondly GholtuTuri had not completed 240 
days in any of the year of his employment since 1989-90 to 2000-01 and therefore he is not entitled for any relief 
under the I.D. Act. He has also relied on and cited judgment of the Hon’ble Supreme Court in case of State of 
Karnataka & Ors Vs. Uma devi and Ors and prayed to reject the reference. 


13. Issue 


Whether the action of the management of BPCL, Dhanbad Depot in terminating the services of Sri 
GholtuTuri, who worked (as Heavy Vehicle Driver) from October, 1989 to 18/10/2002 without following the 
provisions of ID Act, 1947 is just, legal and fair? If not, what relief the workman is entitled to? 


14. As regards the oral testimony of WW-1 LakhanTuri who is the son of Late GholtuTuri, the original 
workman in this case, has deposed that his father was working as Heavy Vehicle Driver at Dhanbad Depot of M/s. 
BPCL and he was appointed in the year 1989 but his service was illegally terminated w.e.f. 18.10.2002. He was 
illegally retrenched from the service of the Company without payment of retrenchment compensation whereas he had 
completed 240 days of attendance each and every year and therefore the termination is illegal and unjustified and he is 
entitled for reinstatement with full back wages. In his cross-examination he has stated that his father was working 
under BPCL since 13 years. He has also admitted that his father was a casual worker. Hence no appointment letter 
was issued to him. 


15. Now as regard the documentary evidence of the workman, on perusal of Exhibit W-1 it transpires that this is 
a letter dated 27.07.1989 issued from the side of the management to the workman GholtuTuri for his written test cum 
interview for Heavy Vehicle Driver II and he was called for interview on 08.08.1989 with direction to bring original 
documents/testimonials regarding age, educational & Technical qualification etc. Exhibit W-2 clearly shows that this 
is a letter dated 18.10.1989 to Sri GholtuTuri from the side of the management regarding casual labour and he was 
called on 30.10.1989 with all the documents/testimonials as required in W-1. Exhibit W-3 is a photo copy of the 
attendance sheet of the month of April 1990 and on its perusal it transpires that the attendance of GholtuTuri have 
been marked in it. Exhibit W-4 and W-5 are related to the Ex-gratia payment for casual workman for the period 2000- 
01 and 01.04.1992 to 31.03.1993 that also bears the name of the workman GholtuTuri along with others. Exhibit W-6 
shows that this is a letter dated 07.08.1983 written to CPM/Calcutta from the side of the management regarding casual 
employment of the workman GholtuTuri and others. Exhibit W-7 is the photo copy of Aadhaar Card of LakhanTuri. 
Exhibit W-8 is the photo copy of Electoral Identity Card of LakhanTuri, the son of GholtuTuri. Exhibit W-9 is the 
Family List Certificate and on its perusal it transpires that the name of LakhanTuri has been shown as the son and 
Fulwa Devi has been shown as the wife of GholtuTuri. Exhibit W-10 is the Death Certificate of the workman 
GholtuTuri and on its perusal it transpires that the date of death is mentioned as 08.04.2011. 


16. Now as regards the oral testimony of MW-1 Sonam Rajgaria, She has deposed that workman was engaged as 
casual employee in their organization. The casual workers are engaged to fill leave vacancy either short or long. They 
were never engaged in any previous year for a period of 240 days or more. In the cross-examination she had admitted 
that after receiving the name of the workmen for employment exchange they went for medical and fitness test and 
found fit. He was given job since 1989 as casual employee and they worked upto Oct 2002 but not continuously. She 
has denied that the workman completed service more than 240 days in the year 1993, 1998 and 1999. She has also 
stated that she cannot say whether any letter issued to the workman not to come to work. 


17. Now, as regards the documentary evidence of the management, on perusal of Exhibit M-1 series it transpires 
that these are the photo copies of the attendance register in 37 pages in which the attendance of the workman 
GholtuTuri has been marked. 


18. On perusal of the oral testimony of WW-1 LakhanTuri as well as MW-1 Sonam Rajgaria as also on perusal 
of Exhibit W-2, W-4 and W-S it is crystal clear that GholtuTuri was appointed as casual workman in BPCL, Dhanbad 
Depot and admittedly he worked since 1989 to 2002 for about 13 years as Heavy Vehicle Driver. The representative 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जनवरी 10, 2026/पौष 20, 1947 7741 


of the workman has claimed and pleaded that GholtuTuri completed more than 240 days of work in every calendar 
year but he has failed to adduce any such documentary evidence in support of his claim. On the other hand the 
management has pleaded that during his entire service tenure the workman did not work 240 days in any of the 
calendar year since 1989 to 2002 and he has also described the same in Tabular Chart in Para-4 of the W/S and in 
support of the same, the management has also adduced Exhibit M-1 Series and on its perusal also it transpires that the 
workman never completed 240 days of the work in any of the calendar year during his entire tenure of service and the 
payment of Ex-gratia is a voluntary gesture of goodwill and made without legal obligation or admission of liability. It 
is separate from wages and does not establish a permanent employment relationship. A casual workman who has not 
completed 240 days of his service in the preceding 12 months is not entitled to the benefit under Industrial Dispute 
Act, 1947 like Section 25F retrenchment compensation and thus he has no relief available under the Act even if he is 
terminated without notice as claimed by the workman. The legal protection under the I.D. Act for retrenchment 
including notice period or pay in lieu, are for the employees who have completed one year of continuous service i.e. 
240 days which is pre-requisite for claiming protection under the Act and if this condition is not met, the workman 
does not qualify for the benefit intended for employees with continuous service of at least one year. 


19. Thus, on the basis of the above discussions of facts, circumstances and evidences available on the case 
record this Tribunal finds and holds that the action of the management of BPCL, Dhanbad Depot in terminating the 
services of Sri GholtuTuri, who worked (as Heavy Vehicle Driver) from October, 1989 to 18/10/2002 without 
following the provisions of ID. Act, 1947 is just, legal and fair and the workman is not entitled to the benefit under 
the provision of I.D. Act, 1947. The reference is answered accordingly. 


Let the copies of Award in duplicate be sent to the Ministry of Labour& Employment, Government of India, 
New Delhi for information and notification. 


SACHINDRA KUMAR PANDEY, Presiding Officer

सरकार बिरला कॉर्पोरेशन लिमिटेड; मेसर्स एच.आर. कॉन्ट्रैक्टर्स; Aad आर.आर. स्टील फैब्रिकेटर्स; Aad बैसवारा

मेसर्स एच.आर. कॉन्ट्रैक्टर के कर्मचारी श्री संदीप सोनकर, मेसर्स बैसवारा सिक्‍योरिटी एजेंसी के कर्मचारी श्री एस.एन.

AWARD 


[PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 


By order No. L-29011/4/2021-IR (M) dated: 12.02.2021 the present industrial dispute has been referred for 
adjudication to this Tribunal in exercise of the powers conferred under Section 36A of the Industrial 


Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947) by the Central Government, with following schedule: 


"WHETHER THE REPRESENTATION DATED 01.01.2020 MADE BY THE REPRESENTATIVE OF 
CONTRACT WORKMEN SH. SUDHIR SINGH AND OTHERS REGARDING INTERPRETATION OF 
SETTLEMENT DATED 11.09.2020 IN TERMS OF ENHANCEMENT OF WAGES W.E.F. 01.08.2020 & 
PAYMENT OF COMPULSORY HOLIDAYS/NATIONAL HOLIDAYS RULES AND CONDITIONS (3 TIMES 
IN CASE THE WORK PERFORMED BY THE WORKMEN) AGAINST M/S BIRLA CORPORATION LTD. 
ARE PROPER & LEGALLY JUSTIFIED? IF SO, AS TO WHAT RELIEF THE CONTRACT WORKMEN 


ARE ENTITLED TO?" 
Accordingly, an Industrial Dispute No. 29/2021 has been registered on 01.03.2021. 


An award dated 27.09.2022, has been passed, relevant portion quoted herein below:- 


For the foregoing reasons, the case is dismissed and; and the workman is not entitled for any relief. 


Thereafter on behalf of claimant in application dated 29|30/09/2022, for recall of the award dated 27.09.2022 has been 
moved, registered as MISC application no. 01/2022 on behalf of respondent, an objection has been filed on 


27.03.2023, (MISC Application no.02/2023). 
The award dated 27.09.2022, was recall and the ID case was disclose to its number. 


Inspite of notice none appeared on behalf of the workman, 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जनवरी 10, 2026/पौष 20, 1947 7743 


I heard Shri AK. Singh learned counsel for respondent, and perused the record. 


From the perusal of record the petition emerged out in last several days i.e. 03.07.2023, 16.10.2023, 05.12.2023, 
22.03.2024, 03.07.2024, and time was granted to file claim statement. 


On 24.09.2024, the matter was fixed for ex parte hearing as the claimant has not filed a claim statement. 
Moreover on 24.12.2024, an order was passed, relevant portion quoted herein below:- 

None for claimant in revised list 

Shri AK. Singh for respondent 

Further one more last opportunity is granted for claim statement. 

List on 24.03.2025. 

Thereafter on 08.06.2025, an order was passed, relevant portion quoted herein below: - 

None for claimant in revised list 

Shri AK. Singh for respondent 

Opportunity to file claim statement is closed 

List on 29.09.2025, for ex parte hearing. 

Today when the matter was taken up neither workman is appeared for any claim statement has been filed. 
FINDINGS & CONCLUSION: 


After hearing the learned counsel and taking into consideration the fact that till date no statement of claim has been 
filed by the claimant in order to establish his claim as per the reference dated 12.02.2021. So in view of the said facts, 
as well as the law laid by the Hon'ble High Court in the case of V. K. Raj Industries v. Labour Court (1) and others 
1981 (29) FLR 194 as under: 


‘Tt is well settled that if a party challenges the legality of an order, the burden lies upon him to prove illegality 
of the order and if no evidence is produced the party invoking jurisdiction of the Court must fail. Whenever a 
workman raises a dispute challenging the validity of the termination of service if is imperative for him to file 
written statement before the Industrial Court setting out grounds on which the order is challenged and he must 
also produce evidence to prove his case. If the workman fails to appear or to file written statement or produce 
evidence, the dispute referred by the State Government cannot be answered in favour of the workman and he 
would not be entitled to any relief." 


In the case of M/s Uptron Powertronics Employees’ Union, Ghaziabad through its Secretary v. Presiding Officer, 
Labour Court (1), Ghaziabad and others 2008 (118) FLR 1164 Hon'ble Allahabad High Court has held as under: 


"The law has been settled by the Apex Court in case of Shanker Chakravarti v. Britannia Biscuit Co. Ltd., V.K. 
Raj Industries v. Labour Court and Ors., Airtech Private Limited v. State of U.P. and Ors. 1984 (49) FLR 38 
and Meritech India Ltd. v. State of U.P. and Ors. 1996 FLR that in the absence of any evidence led by or on 
behalf of the workman the reference is bound to be answered by the court against the workman. In such a 
situation it is not necessary for the employers to lead any evidence at all. The obligation to lead evidence to 
establish an allegation made by a party is on the party making the allegation. The test would be, who would fail 
if no evidence is led." 

And by the Hon'ble Allahabad High Court in the case of District Administrative Committee, U.P. P.A.C.C.S.C. 

Services v. Secretary-cum- G.M. District Co-operative Bank Ltd. 2010 (126) FLR 519; wherein it has been held as 

under: 


"The submission is that even if the petitioner failed to lead the evidence, burden was on the shoulders of the 
respondent to prove the termination order as illegal. He was required to lead evidence first which he failed. A 
perusal of the impugned award also does not show that any evidence either oral or documentary was led by the 
respondent. In the case of no evidence, the reference has to be dismissed." 


As the workman has not filed any statement of claim/oral/documentary evidence, so the present case is liable to be 
dismissed. 


AWARD 
For the foregoing reasons, the case is dismissed and; and the workman is not entitled for any relief. 
Award as above. 
Lucknow, 
18 October 2025.

अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट केन्द्रीय सरकार औद्योगिक अधिकरण एवं श्रम न्यायालय, जबलपुर, पंचाट (रिफरेन्स न.- 24/2021)

[No. Z-16025/04/2025-IR(M)-167]

Management 

(JUDGMENT) 
(Passed on this 187 day of November, 2025) 


As per letter dated 18/03/2021 by the Government of India, Ministry of Labour, New Delhi, the reference 
has been made to this Tribunal under Section-10 of Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (in short the ‘Act’)as per 
Notification No. J-1(5-1)/2021-IR dt. 18/03/2021. The dispute under reference relates to: 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जनवरी 10, 2026/पौष 20, 1947 7745 


“क्या प्रबंधन वरिष्ठ प्रबंधक: भ्रारतीय जीवन बीमा निगम; जबन्रपुर व शाखा प्रबंधक; भारतीय जीवन बीमा निगम; 
बानाघाट एवं श्री धनेश चोरवाडे श्रमिक के मध्य Heine विवाव हैं? यवि हाँ तब क्या कायित रुप से प्रबंधन के माँखिक 
आवेश CART त्री धनेश चोखाडे कौ कार्य पर न रखकर उस़के स्थान पर किसी अन्य व्यक्ति को कार्य पर रखते हुए उसका 
(धनेश चोरवाडे सेवा से निष्कासन क्या न्यायोचित हैं? यवि नहीं तो HATH किस अनुतोष का अधिकारी हैं?" 


The case of the workman is that, he was appointed as a labour by management of LIC on 01.04.2009 and 
worked continuously till 01.04.2017 when he was disengaged without any notice or compensation which is against 
the act. 


Case of management is that, he was a daily wager engaged on as and when required basis who never 
worked for 240 days in any year. He worked for only | month in 2009, 9 months in 2010, 12 months in 2011, 5 
months in 2004, 1 month in 2014, 6 months in 2015, 4 months in 2016 and 2 months in 2017. It is also the case of the 
management that he was an outsourced employee deputed by the outsourcing agency. He became mentally unstable 
and the outsourcing agency was asked by management to replace him. The outsourcing agency replaced the workman 
and deputed some other person. Thus, according to management, the workman was not employed by them. Hence 
there is no question of his disengagement by management of LIC. 


In evidence, the workman did not appear and did not file any evidence. Management filed an affidavit of its 
witness as his Examination-in-chief, he has been cross-examined by workman side. 


I have heard argument of Learned Counsel for workman Mr. Praveen Yadav, Learned Counsel for 
Management of LIC, Mr. Amitabh Bharti. I have gone through the record as well. 


The reference itself is the issue for determination. 


The initial burden to prove his case that firstly, he was appointed by the LIC and secondly, he worked for 
240 days in the year preceding the date of his disengagement as well also that he was disengaged by LIC is on 
workman. He has not filed any evidence in this respect. Hence, he is held to have failed in discharging this burden. On 
the other hand, management witness has corroborated the case of LIC and there is nothing in his cross-examination to 
discredit him. 


Hence, on the basis of above discussion and findings, holding the claim of the workman not proved, the 
reference stands answered against the workman. 


No order as to cost. 
DATE:- 18-11-2025 
P.K. SRIVASTAVA, Presiding Officer 


नई दिल्‍ली, 30 दिसम्बर, 2025

The General Manager,

The Station Manager. 

Jet Airways (India) Ltd. Airport, 
Khajuraho Airport, Distt. Khajuraho, 
Chattarpur, Madhya Pradesh-471001 


Management 


(JUDGMENT) 
(Passed on this 7" day of November, 2025) 


As per letter dated 28/10/2022 by the Government of India, Ministry of Labour, New Delhi, the reference 
has been made to this Tribunal under Section-10 of Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (in short the ‘Act’)as per 
Notification No. J-8(27-B)/2018-ALC dt. 28/10/2022. The dispute under reference relates to: 


“Whether the action of the Management of Khajuraho Tours Khajuraho through its proprietor/Manager 
and Jet airways Airlines through its Station Manager in terminating the services of Shri Babulal Raikwar 
S/o Late Chiddi Raikwar, Ex- Casual Labour (UAN No.100104742220, who was appointed on 1/1/2000 
and continuously worked more than 16 years up to 1/5/2017) without complying with the relevant 
provisions of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 is fair, legal and justified? If not, to what relief the 
concerned workman is entitled and from which date?” 


The case of the workman is mainly that, he worked in the establishment of M/s Jet Airways Airlines at 
Khajuraho Airport as an employee of M/s Khajuraho Tours since 01.01.2020 till date of his disengagement on 
12.02.2019 as helper. He was retrenched without any notice or compensation which is in violation of section 25-F and 
25-G of the Act. This is unfair labour practice adopted by the management, hence the action of management is unjust, 
illegal and arbitrary. He has further alleged that his work was of permanent and perennial nature involving loading 
and unloading of cargo. He raised dispute against his retrenchment before the concerned Labour Commissioner who 
refused to refer the dispute. Thereafter he filed a writ petition No. 9124/2019 before Hon’ble High Court of M.P. and 
it was under order of Hon’ble High Court on 12.02.2019, the reference was made to this Tribunal. He has prayed that 
holding his disengagement against law, he be held entitled to be reinstated with all back wages and benefits. 


Case of M/s Khajuraho Tours, who appeared and filed its written statement of defense , is that the 
workman was engaged by them and was deputed to work with the establishment of M/s Jet Airways Airlines in 
outsourcing contract, which was allotted by M/s Jet Airways Airlines to M/s Khajuraho Tours. He worked till 30 
April, 2017 and submitted his resignation on 30.04.2017. He had been given all his dues including provident fund 
after his resignation was accepted since, then he left the institution of M/s Khajuraho Tours. Hence, there is no 
occasion of termination of his services as claimed by him. 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जनवरी 10, 2026/पौष 20, 1947 7747 


The management of M/s Khajuraho Tours has prayed that the reference be answered against the workman. 
None appeared for M/s Jet Airways Airlines inspite of service. 
During pendency, the workman died and his legal representative, his widow was substituted in his place. 


In evidence, the workman did not prove any document, no affidavit from the side of the workman was filed 
in support to his case. 


M/s Khajuraho Tours has filed affidavit of its partner Jitendra Lavaniya as his Examination-in-chief, he was 
cross-examined by workman side. The management proved photocopy of resignation of the workman as Ex-M/1. 


I have heard argument of Learned Counsel for the workman Mrs. Poonam Daharwal, Learned Counsel for 
the Management was not available at the time of argument, they did not file any written argument. I have gone 
through the record also. 


The initial burden to prove his case lies on the workman in absence of his any proved document or statement 
on oath, he is held to have failed in discharging this burden. On the other hand, the management witness has 
corroborated their case and proved the resignation letter of the workman, there is nothing in cross-examination of the 
management witness to discredit him. 


On the basis of above discussion and findings, holding the claim of the workman not proved, the reference 
deserves to be answered as follows:- 


AWARD 


“Holding the action of the Management of Khajuraho Tours Khajuraho through its proprietor/Manager 
in terminating the services of Shri Babulal Raikwar S/o Late Chiddi Raikwar, Ex- Casual Labour 
(UAN No. 100104742220, fair, legal and justified, he is held entitled to no relief. 


No order as to cost. 
DATE:- 07-11-2025 


P.K. SRIVASTAVA, Presiding Officer 
नई दिल्‍ली, 30 दिसम्बर, 2025

सरकार मेसर्स भारतीय जीवन बीमा निगम के प्रबंधतंत्र के संबद्ध नियोजकों और भारतीय जीवन बीमा निगम कर्मचारी 
संघ के बीच अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट केन्द्रीय सरकार औद्योगिक अधिकरण एवं श्रम न्यायालय, जबलपुर, Tare (रिफरेन्स 
न.- 24/2022) को जैसा कि अनुलग्रक में दिखाया गया है, प्रकाशित करती है जो केन्द्रीय सरकार को सॉफ्ट कॉपी के साथ

Government hereby publishes the award (Reference No. 24/2022) of the Central Government Industrial Tribunal

1748 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JANUARY 10, 2026/PAUSHA 20, 1947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)]

THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL CUM

H.J.S.(Retd) 
The General Secretary, 
Bhartiya Jeevan Bima Nigam Karmachari Sangh, 
594/8, Nehru Nagar, M.I.G. Thana Main Raod, 
Indore (M.P.) - 452011 


Workman 
Versues 

The General Manager, 

M/s Bhartiya Jeevan Bima Nigam, 

Central Regional Office, 

B, Hoshangabad Road, 

Bhopal (M.P.) - 452060 

Management 
(JUDGMENT) 


(Passed on this 12" day of November - 2025) 


As per letter dated 25/05/2022 of the Government of India, Ministry of Labour, New Delhi, the reference is 
made to this Tribunal under Section-10 of Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (in short the ‘Act’)as per Notification 
No. L- 17011/05/2022 (IR(M)) dt. 25/02/2022. The dispute under reference relates to: 


“Whether the demand of the Madhya Pradesh Bhartiya Jeevan Bima Nigam Karmachari Sangh (Intuc) 
Indore, M.P. for temporary allowance to workers working in the management M/s Bhartiya Jeevan Bima 
Nigam is legal, fair and justified? If not, what other relief the workmen are entitled to?” 


After registering the case on the basis of reference Notices were issued to both the parties. They appeared 
and filed their respective statement of claim and defense. 


Case of the Workman union is mainly that, they are a registered union under the Trade Union Act affiliated 
INTUC bearing Registration No. 6892 and the General Secretary of the Union has been authorized to prosecute the 
claim on behalf of its workers who are working in different branches of the Management of LIC (Life Insurance 
Corporation) as temporary employees against vacant and regular posts since 2017 continuously and regularly. For the 
job which are permanent and perennial in nature. They are being paid their wages on the basis of wages fixed by the 
Central Government for Daily Employees on monthly basis. They have been extended benefits of ESIC etc. which 
they are affiliating. On February 15, 1987 guidelines issued by Central Office of LIC according to which the 
temporary employees who were employed for month or more, were directed to be paid same wages as drawn by the 
regular employees at minimum of the of the scale applicable to them who have been appointed with LIC for 
discharging same nature of duties. The members of the Union are working as sweepers and peons of the LIC. The 
Management of LIC in Bhopal region did not extend the benefit of the said Circular, though it was extended to the 
same Class of Employees working on temporary basis in Amravati Division. A dispute was raised in this respect by 
the Union before the Labour Commissioner which could not be conciliated, hence this reference. 


According to Workmen Union this action of Management of LIC Bhopal region in not extending benefits 
of the Circular dated 25.02.1987 to the members of the Union who are discharging the same duties of sweepers and 
peons as temporary workers which are discharged by the regular appointed sweepers and peons is nothing but illegal, 
unjust and arbitrary and is in violation of Article 14 of the Constitution of India. The Union has thus prayed that 
holding its applicant members who are temporary employees as mentioned above, entitled to same benefit with 
respect to pay by way of granting the minimum of pay-scale to its regularly appointed employees discharging the 
same duties. The Management of LIC Bhopal Region be directed to grant the same to the applicants. 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जनवरी 10, 2026/पौष 20, 1947 7749 


Workman has filed copy of registration certificate, copy of application and documents submitted by the 
Union before the Concerned Labour Commissioner with list of the principle employees, copies of the guidelines dated 
25.02.1987 copy of payment vouchers of temporary employees of Amravati and Indore Division. 


The case of LIC, as taken by them in their Written Statement of defense, is that they have taken a decision 
to outsource all its none core activities all over MP, challenged before the Hon’ble High Court of MP by the Union 
by way of filing Writ Petition No. 17420/2024, and was dismissed after hearing by the Hon’ble High Court vide its 
order dated 17.08.2023 and the decision of discharging for all none core activities by the Management was affirmed. 
Hence, at present this work is being done by contractors like Jai Balaji Security etc. and not by the LIC. Hence, the 
issue with respect to payment of salary and wages as claimed by the Workman Union loses relevance. According to 
the Management, the guidelines issued by the Central Office on 25.02.1987 regarding wages and other conditions 
applicable to the temporary workmen were only with respect to the those temporary workmen who were covered 
under the Award passed by the National Industrial Tribunal on 17.04.1986 in Reference Case No. NTB-01 of 1985 
and was with respect to the temporary workmen who were covered under the said Award. These guidelines have 
nothing to do with the present appellants. This is also the case of the Management of LIC that as per order dated 
18.01.2011 of Hon’ble High Court in Civil Appeal No. 953-968/2005 Life Insurance Corporation of India V.s. D.V. 
Anil Kumar, one time scheme dated 20.05.2011 was framed and daily wager workers who fulfilled the eligibility 
criteria as per this One Time Scheme, were regularized. The rest who were not eligible to be regularized were 
discontinued and at present a decision was taken to discharge all none core activities all over M.P. Circle which was 
affirmed by Hon’ble High Court of MP as mentioned above. The Outsourcing Agency has hired the Applicant 
Workmen and has deputed them in various offices for cleaning etc. Their wages are paid by Outsourcing Agencies 
and not by LIC. Also it has been appealed that, Management of LIC never issued any appointment letter to these 
claimants. According to LIC (Life Insurance Corporation of India) benefit of Temporary Instructions 1993 effective 
from 28.06.1993 these applicants who worked under the Offices of MP Circle of LIC are not temporary staff because 
they are not appointed by any Class- IV post rather they were hired as Daily Wager/Casual Labours. It is further the 
case of the Management is that, question of similar treatment to daily wager with temporary staff arise in the light of 
the said instructions of 1993. 


In the light of these submissions, the issue which arises to be considered is whether the members of 
Workmen Union, who are undisputedly the outsourced employees engaged by the Management of LIC, Bhopal 
Division through outsourcing agencies, deserve to be treated as temporary employee of LIC or not? 


Life Insurance Corporation of India (Staff Rules) 1960, Chapter 2, Clause 5 provides that staff of the 
corporation shall be classified as Officers, Development Officers, Supervisors and Clerical Staff and Sub-ordinate 
Staff. Clause 8 of the Rules provides for an employment of staff in Class III and Class IV on temporary basis. Since, 
the members of the Union are not employed by Management of LIC as temporary staff, rather they are outsourced 
employees deputed by Outsourcing Company after employing them, hence they cannot be held to be entitle to the 
benefits admissible to the Temporary Employees. 


The Circular dated 25.02.1987 is on record. This circular deals with charges of employee workman, part- 
time Workman and Badli Workman. These are employed by the LIC itself. Hence, the benefits under the said circular 
cannot be granted to those employees who are deputed as outsourced employees with the LIC. 


On the basis of above discussion and findings, the claim of the Workman Union with respect to 
benefits to the temporary workers working with the Management of LIC Bhopal region as per Circular dated 
25.05.1987 has no leg to stand. 


Before parting, reference of The Rule 25 of the Contract Labour (Regulation and Abolition) Rules, 1971, 
particularly, Rule 25 (v-(a)) of Contract Labour (Regulation and Abolition) Rules, 1971,is necessary which is 
being reproduced requires to be referred to, which is as follows: 


“(v)(a) In cases where the workmen employed by the contractor perform the same or similar 
kind of work as the workmen directly employed by the principal employer of the 
establishment, the wage rates, holidays, hours of work and other condition of service of 
the workmen of the contractor shall be the same as applicable to the workmen directly 
employed by the principal employer of the establishment on the same or similar kind of 
work: 


Provided that in the case of any disagreement with regard to the type of work 
the same shall be decided by the Chief Labour Commissioner (Central).” 


The Union is at liberty to raise their claim under these Rules before an appropriate forum. 
On the basis of above discussion and findings, the reference is answered as follows. 
AWARD 


Holding the demand of the Madhya Pradesh Bhartiya Jeevan Bima Nigam Karmachari Sangh (INTUC) 
Indore, M.P. for temporary allowance to the workers working with Management of M/s Bhartiya Jeevan Bima 
Nigam as outsourced employees is held unjustified and the members are held entitled to no relief. 


7150 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JANUARY 10, 2026/PAUSHA 20, 1947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


Further, they are at liberty to raise their claim before an appropriate forum in the light of Rule 25 (v-(a)) 
of Contract Labour (Regularization and Abolition) Rules, 1971. 


No order as to cost. 
DATE:- 12/11/2025 
P.K. SRIVASTAVA, Presiding Officer 


नई दिल्‍ली, 30 दिसम्बर, 2025

सरकार हिर्री माइंस सेल के प्रबंधतंत्र के संबद्ध नियोजकों और हिंदुस्तान स्टील कर्मचारी संघ के बीच अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट 
केन्द्रीय सरकार औद्योगिक अधिकरण एवं श्रम न्यायालय, जबलपुर, Tare (रिफरेन्स न.- 03/2024) को जैसा कि

का.आ. 2271.---औद्योगिक विवाद अधिनियम, 1947 (1947 का 14) की धारा 17 के अनुसरण में, केन्द्रीय 
सरकार एसीसी लिमिटेड के प्रबंधतंत्र के संबद्ध नियोजकों और श्री संजय तिवारी के बीच अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट केन्द्रीय सरकार 
औद्योगिक अधिकरण एवं श्रम न्यायालय, जबलपुर, पंचाट (रिफरेन्स न.- 50/2025) को जैसा कि अनुलग्नरक में दिखाया 


गया है, प्रकाशित करती है जो केन्द्रीय सरकार को सॉफ्ट कॉपी के साथ 30.12.2025 को प्राप्त हुआ था |

under reference related to :- 


हर Whether Mr. Sanjay Tiwari, Deputy Manager (Grade 0-4) is a workman or not as per Industrial 
Dispute Act, 1947. 


2 If answer to 1 is in affirmative then whether the termination from service is legal and justified ? 
If no, what relief the applicant, is entitled to ?” 


After registering a case on the basis of the reference, notices were sent to the parties and served. Mr. Rahul 
Mishra learned Counsel filed his Vakalatnama on behalf of Management.

withdraw the reference on the ground that as per legal advice, he may not be a workman under the Act. In the 
light of the withdrawal application, the reference becomes infructuous and is answered accordingly. The 
applicant is at liberty to approach proper Forum for redressal of his grievance.
