C.G.1LT-cum-L.C., Asansol. 
REFERENCE CASE NO. 10 OF 2023 
PARTIES: Manoj Hari 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जनवरी 10, 2026/पौष 20, 1947 7823 


Vs 

Management of Khas Kajora Colliery, Kajora Area, ECL 
REPRESENTATIVES: 
For the Union/Workman: Mr. Rakesh Kumar, President, Koyala Mazdoor Congress 
For the Management of ECL: Mr. P. K. Das, Advocate. 
INDUSTRY: Coal. 
STATE: West Bengal. 
Dated: 17/10/2025 


AWARD 


In exercise of powers conferred under clause (d) of Sub-section (1) and Sub-section (2A) of Section 10 of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Government of India through the Ministry of Labour, vide its Order 
No. L-22012/07/2023-IR(CM-ID) dated 01/02/2023 has been pleased to refer the following dispute between the 
employer, that is the Management of Khas Kajora Colliery, Kajora Area of Eastern Coalfields Limited and their 
workman for adjudication by this Tribunal. 


“Whether the action of the management of Khas Kajora Colliery, Kajora Area of M/s. E.C.Ltd. in dismissal 
from service to Sri Manoj Hadi, Ex-General Mazdoor, U.Man No. 196919 vide order No, ECL/KA/ APM/C- 
6/2018/20/2510 dated 13/03/2018 is proper, legal and justified? If not, what relief the workman concerned is 
entitled to and what directions are necessary to the management in this respect?” 


1 On receiving Order No. L-22012/07/2023-IR(CM-II) dated 01/02/2023 from the Government of India 
Ministry of Labour, New Delhi for adjudication of the dispute, a Reference case No. 10 of 2023 was registered on 
01/02/2023 and an order was passed for issuing notice to the parties through registered post, directing them to appear 
and submit their written statements along with relevant documents in support of their claims. 


2. Koyala Mazdoor Congress, representative union filed written statement on 05/04/2023 stating therein that 
Manoj Hari, General Mazdoor, U. Man No. 196919 was a permanent employee of ECL at Khas Kajora Colliery. Due 
to illness, he could not attend his duty from 14/07/2016. After recovery from illness when he reported for duty, he 
submitted treatment papers but the management did not allow him to join. A charge sheet bearing No. KKC/P&IR/C- 
6/CS/17/504 dated 17/04/2017 was issued against him over the charges of unauthorized absence from duty for more 
than ten days and for his habitual absence. Manoj Hari replied to the charge sheet, stating that he was undergoing 
medical treatment at Sub-Divisional Hospital, Asansol and in support of his claim, he submitted treatment papers. 
Management initiated a domestic enquiry and the workman participated in the enquiry proceeding. At the time of 
enquiry, he once again replied the fact that due to illness, he was unable to attend duty and that he had no intention to 
remain away from duty. Treatment papers were produced before the Enquiry Officer to show that remaining absent 
from duty was beyond his control on account of his illness. Enquiry Officer on concluding enquiry submitted his 
report to the management of the colliery. A second show cause Notice was issued to which the workman replied 
requesting the management to allow him to join duty. On recommendation of the Agent of the Colliery, General 
Mazdoor of the Area issued an order of dismissal dated 13/03/2018 dismissing the workman from service of the 
company. 


3. After receiving order of dismissal, Manoj Hari, the dismissed workman submitted a mercy appeal before the 
Director (Pers), ECL Headquarters under the provision of Certified Standing Orders of the company and also in 
accordance with the Memorandum of Settlement dated 22/05/2007 which was agreed upon between the management 
and workmen in presence of RLC (C), Asansol. Workman submitted two mercy appeals dated 20/04/2018 and 
25/09/2018 praying for his reinstatement in service. Management of ECL did not reply to the Mercy Appeals. This 
incident was followed by Covid pandemic. It is submitted by the union that workman was absent from duty for about 
nine months from 14/07/2016 to 17/04/2017 and for such absence due to illness, Management should not have 
awarded the highest punishment of dismissal. It is contended that nature of punishment is disproportionate to the 
alleged misconduct and the union prayed for setting aside the order of dismissal passed against the workman, along 
with payment of back wages and consequential benefits. 


4 Management contested the case by filing written statement on 05/04/2023. According to the management 
Manoj Hari, the dismissed workman was posted as a General Mazdoor and his date of birth is 24/10/1983. He was 
appointed in service of ECL on 31/03/2011 at Khas Kajora Colliery. Workman was a habitual absentee and he was 
reprimanded on several occasions. Workman was absent from duty from 14/07/2016 and continued his unauthorized 
absence without providing information to the appropriate authority. Management of ECL issued charge sheet dated 
17/04/2017 under clauses 26.23 and 26.29 of Certified Standing Orders applicable to Coal Industries for habitual late 


7824 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JANUARY 10, 2026/PAUSHA 20, 1947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


attendance or habitual absence from duty without sufficient cause and absence from duty for more than ten days 
without sanctioned leave or sufficient cause respectively. A domestic enquiry was initiated against the workman. 
Three Notice of enquiry were served upon the workman but he did not turn up after first two Notices and finally 
workman participated in the enquiry held on 26/09/2017. Enquiry Officer followed the principle of Natural Justice 
and provided opportunity to the charged employee to defend himself. In course of enquiry, the charge of unauthorized 
absence was proved against the workman beyond doubt. A second show cause Notice was issued on 31/10/2017 and 
the charged employee submitted his reply to it but the same was not found satisfactory. On the basis of findings of 
Enquiry Officer, the General Manager, Kajora Area dismissed the workman from service of the company by issuing a 
letter No. ECL/KA/APM/C-6/2018/10/2510 dated 13/03/2018. Further case of the management is that workman was 
a habitual absentee and his past record shows that he attended duty for seventy two days in the year 2013, eighty three 
days in the year 2014, fifty five days in the year 2015 and forty four days in the year 2016 (upto 13/07/2016). It is 
urged by the management that unauthorized absence from duty is a serious misconduct and it hampers work of the 
employer and affects the production process. Workman was given ample opportunity to rectify himself but he did not 
amend his nature. Management relied upon the settled position of law that habitual absence means gross violation of 
discipline and habitual absence is a factor establishing lack of interest of the employee in work. According to the 
management workman must not conduct his duty in this negligent manner and perform work responsibly not only for 
personal excellence but also for collective good of the organization which he serves. Management urged that its action 
in dismissing the workman is totally lawful, proper and the delinquent is not entitled to any relief. 


5. Moot point for consideration in the reference is whether the action of the management in dismissing Manoj 
Hari, General Mazdoor from service is proper, legal and justified? If not what relief the workman is entitled to? 


6. Manoj Hari filed his affidavit-in-chief and examined himself as Workman Witness-I. He produced the 
following documents in support of his case: 


(i) copy of charge sheet issued to the workman is marked as Exhibit W-1. 

(ii) copy of reply submitted by the workman against the charge sheet is marked as Exhibit W-2. 

(ili) copy of medical documents in support of illness issued from the Asansol District Hospital is marked as 
Exhibit W-3. 


(iv) copy of OPD treatment card is marked as Exhibit W-4. 


(५) copy of written explanation submitted against the second show cause Notice in two pages is collectively 
marked as Exhibit W-5. 


(vi) copy of letter issued to the workman by the General Manager, Kajora Area, terminating him from service 
w.e.f. 08/03/2018 is marked as Exhibit W-6. 


(vii) copy of mercy appeals submitted by the workman on 20/04/2018 and 25/09/2018 for his reinstatement in 
service are marked as Exhibits W-7 and W-8 respectively. 


During cross-examination workman witness stated that he used to attend duty from Andal and did not get 
medical treatment from the company hospital during his long absence of nine months. It transpires from the testimony 
of WW-I that he was admitted to Asansol District Hospital and produced documents. Workman stated that he reported 
for duty one month after he was discharged from the hospital and informed the reason of absence. Workman admitted 
that he was absent from duty from 14/07/2016 to 14/04/2017. He also admitted that prior to issuance of charge sheet, 
he could not attend duty on regular basis. In reply to the suggestion he was not suffering from illness, witness denied 
the same. He further deposed that he did not intentionally remain away from his place of work and stated that he was 
not dismissed from service on proper ground and for proper reasons. Averments made by the workman that he 
submitted two mercy petitions dated 20/04/2018 and 25/09/2018 for allowing him to join duty as per Memorandum of 
Settlement dated 22/05/2007 has not been refuted by the Management. 


7. In the instant case management examined Mr. Proloy Dasgupta as Management Witness-I. He filed an 
affidavit-in-chief where he alleged that the workman was a habitual absentee and was previously reprimanded on 
several occasions for his habitual absence. MW-I further deposed that the workman participated in the enquiry after 
third Notice of 


Enquiry and was given fully opportunity to defend himself. It is gathered from the testimony of MW-I that charge of 
unauthorized absence was proved beyond doubt and a second show cause Notice was issued to him on 31/10/2017. It 
appears from the admission that charged employee submitted reply to the second show cause Notice and the 
management finally dismissed the workman. In course of examination, witness produced the following documents: 


(i) Copy of charge sheet dated 17/04/2017 issued against the workman is marked as Exhibit M-1 


(ii) Copy of reply to the charge sheet submitted by the workman along with medical certificates from Asansol 
District Hospital in four pages is collectively marked as Exhibit M-2. 


[भाग त--खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जनवरी 10, 2026/पौष 20, 1947 7825 


(iii) Copy of letter of appointment of the Enquiry Officer is marked as Exhibit M-3. 


(iv) Copy of first Notice of Enquiry dated 26/06/2017 is marked as Exhibit M-4 and copy of postal receipt is 
marked as Exhibit M-4/1. 


(v) Copy of second Notice of Enquiry dated 31/07/2017 is marked as Exhibit M-5 and copy of postal receipt is 
marked as Exhibit M-5/1. 


(vi) Copy of third Notice of Enquiry dated 12/09/2017 which was received by the workman is marked as 
Exhibit M-6. 


(vii) Copy of enquiry proceeding dated 26/09/2017 in two pages is collectively marked as Exhibit M-7. 
(viii) | Copy of enquiry report is marked as Exhibit M-8. 
(ix) Copy of second show cause Notice served upon the workman is marked as Exhibit M-9. 


(x) Copy of reply submitted by the workman against the second show cause Notice in two pages is collectively 
marked as Exhibit M-10. 


(xi) Copy of letter of dismissal issued by the General Manager, Kajora Area dated 13/03/2018 is marked as 
Exhibit M-11. 


In cross-examination, Management Witness admitted that medical treatment papers were submitted by the 
workman in course of enquiry. In respect of earlier enquiries, no order of punishment was filed. He also admitted that 
mercy appeal submitted by the dismissed workman was not considered. Management Witness denied that punishment 
imposed upon the workman was disproportionate to the nature of his misconduct. 


8. Mr. Rakesh Kumar, union representative arguing the case on behalf of the dismissed workman submitted that 
the workman participated in the enquiry proceeding and produced documents in support of his illness which have 
been marked as Exhibit W-3, Exhibit W-4 and Exhibit M-2 which proved beyond doubt that the workman was 
suffering from illness during the period of his absence for a period of nine months. It is further argued that though the 
charge was levelled against the employee for his unauthorized absence as well as habitual absence, there is no finding 
in respect of the charge of habitual absence. It is submitted that the Enquiry Officer on the basis of evidence adduced 
before him held that the charged employee was guilty for unauthorized absence. According to the union representative 
a second show cause Notice was issued to the charged workman and he submitted reply addressed to the Agent which 
has been marked as Exhibit W-5. In reply to the second show cause Notice the workman stated that he was suffering 
from serious illness from 14/07/2016 to 17/04/2017. He also denied that he was a habitual absentee and late-comer. 
Mr. Kumar argued that though charge of habitual absence was not proved and allegation in the second show cause 
Notice was also denied in reply which has been produced as Exhibit W-5/Exhibit M-10, the General Manager issued 
order of dismissal presuming that the charge of habitual absence has been proved against the workman beyond doubt. 
It is argued that though the charge of habitual absence was not proved and there is no finding of the Enquiry Officer 
over such charge, the appropriate authority without consideration of such fact and findings has misplaced his decision 
by presuming that both charges were proved. Regarding charge of unauthorized absence, Mr. Kumar argued that it is 
true that the workman did not inform the management about his absence for nine months but there are sufficient 
reasons for his absence from duty as he was suffering from illness and produced medical documents in support of the 
same. Union representative contended that the workman was thirty two years of age at the time of his dismissal from 
service and according to the Memorandum of Settlement date 22/05/2007 management of ECL accepted that if period 
of absence was within nine months and the workman was within the age of limit of forty five years, the management 
would consider the mercy appeal praying for reinstatement in service. In the instant case after dismissal, the workman 
submitted two mercy petitions praying for reinstatement in service but the management admittedly did not consider 
the same till date. Union representative on such ground prayed for setting aside the order of dismissal and 
reinstatement of the workman in the service of the company. 


9. Mr. P. K. Das, learned advocate for the management of ECL argued that the workman did not inform the 
management about his suffering from illness and remained absent without any authorization of leave. Referring to the 
reply submitted by the workman in response to charge sheet (Exhibit W-2/Exhibit M-2), ld. Advocate submitted that 
the workman admitted guilt of his unauthorized absence. A fair departmental enquiry was held in which the workman 
participated and was found guilty over the charge of unauthorized absence for more than ten days. It is argued that the 
management lost confidence on the workman due to his indisciplined nature, which adversely affected the interest of 
the management. Ld. Advocate urged that on earlier occasion the workman habitually remained absent from the year 
2013 to 2016 (upto 13/07/2016) for which he was reprimanded but the workman did not rectify himself. It is argued 
that after considering materials placed before the Disciplinary Authority, final decision was taken for dismissal of the 
workman from service. It is asserted that the punishment imposed against the workman is proper and proportionate to 
the nature of misconduct. Therefore, there is no reason to interfere with the finding of the Enquiry Officer and the 
Industrial Dispute is liable to be dismissed. 


7826 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JANUARY 10, 2026/PAUSHA 20, 1947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


10. I have considered the rival arguments of the union as well as of the management and also assessed the 
evidence on record in the light of fact and circumstances of the case. Admittedly Manoj Hari, a permanent employee 
of ECL who was appointed in service on 31/03/2011 remained absent from duty without authorization continuously 
for nine months from 14/07/2016 to 17/04/2017. Management of ECL issued charge sheet against him disclosing the 
charge under clause 26.29 and 26.23 of the Certified Standing Orders for unauthorized absence from duty for more 
than ten days without sufficient cause and habitual absence from duty respectively. After issuance of charge sheet the 
workman submitted reply (Exhibit W-2/Exhibit M-2) where he stated that he was under medical treatment at Asansol 
District Hospital and enclosed treatment papers in the name of Manoj Hari. Management was not satisfied with the 
reply and initiated a domestic enquiry against him. It may be derived from evidence of the management that the 
workman was undergoing medical treatment at Asansol District Hospital on 14/07/2016 as well as on 12/12/2016. 
Medical certificate issued by the Medical Officer (Exhibit M-2) reveals that Manoj Hari was receiving medical 
treatment at Asansol District Hospital from 14/07/2016 as OPD patient with various ailments. In my considered view 
the Enquiry Officer failed to consider the medical papers as there was sufficient cause for absence of workman during 
the said period. Medical documents have not been challenged in the course of evidence before the Enquiry Officer. 
Therefore, no shadow of doubt is cast over the union’s claim that the workman was suffering from illness. It is 
apparent from evidence on record that the workman was prevented from attending duty during the period of his 
absence due to his illness, which was beyond his control. In the instant case, Enquiry Officer did not hold the charged 
employee guilty over the charge of habitual absence. In the second show cause Notice (Exhibit M-9) Agent, Khas 
Kajora Colliery directed the charged employee to respond to the charge of habitual absence proved against the 
workman during the year 2013-2016 despite the fact that the Enquiry Officer did not arrive at any finding on the 
charge of habitual absence. In the enquiry report (Exhibit M-8), Enquiry Officer held that “in view of the above I 
found that the charges of unauthorized absence levelled against Sri Manoj Hari, UG General Mazdoor, U. M. No. 
196919 of Khas Kajora Colliery as per Certified Standing Order are proved beyond doubt. I submit my enquiry report 
to the Colliery Manager, Khas Kajora Colliery.” In the finding of Enquiry Officer, there is no reference to the charge 
of habitual absence therefore, in order to find the charged employee guilty over the charge of habitual absence, 
Disciplinary Authority was required to issue a Notice to show cause to the charged employee informing him that he 
differed with the finding of the Enquiry Officer on such point and on the basis of evidence on record, he held a 
different view that the charged employee was found guilty of habitual absence also. In absence of such procedure 
adopted, the Disciplinary Authority could not have found Manoj Hari guilty of the second charge. I find from the 
record that the workman was suffering from illness at the relevant time but the finding of the Enquiry Officer was 
perverse and is not consistent with the evidence on record when he found the workman guilty on the charge of 
unauthorized absence though there was sufficient ground for absence of the workman from duty. The workman after 
his dismissal submitted two mercy petitions dated 20/04/2018 and 25/09/2018 for his reinstatement in service. 
Though the applications were made on the basis of Memorandum of Settlement dated 22/05/2007 and the workman 
fulfilled pre-conditions that the period of absence should be less than nine months and the workman was below the 
age of forty five years, management neither considered the same nor did they respond to such representations. From 
the fact and circumstances of this case and the evidence adduced, 1 find that the workman is about forty two years of 
age now and still has several years of work left. It appears to me that the order of dismissal dismissing the workman 
from service due to absence during his illness is a clear violation of the principle of Natural Justice. Management 
ought to have consider the reason for which the workman could not attend duty. Evidence produced by the workman 
cannot be overlooked, especially when such documents and evidence have not been challenged at the time of cross- 
examination of the witness. In such view of matter, order of dismissal passed against the workman is arbitrary, 
improper and not sustainable under law. Accordingly order of dismissal dated 13/03/2018 issued by the General 
Manager, Kajora Area terminating the service of Manoj Hari, General Mazdoor is set aside. Management is directed 
to reinstate the workman in his employment under ECL within two months from the date of communication of this 
order. Management shall also pay thirty percent of back wages to the workman from the date of his dismissal till his 
reinstatement in service. 


Hence, 
ORDERED 


that the Industrial Dispute is allowed on contest. Order of dismissal dated 13/03/2018 passed by the General Manager, 
Kajora Area dismissing Manoj Hari from service of the company is set aside. Management is directed to reinstate the 
workman within two months from the communication of this order, maintaining continuity of service of the workman. 
Management shall also pay thirty percent of back wages to the workman from the date of his dismissal i.e. 08/03/2018 
till his reinstatement in service and he shall also be entitled to the consequential benefits. Let an Award be drawn up 
in the light of my above discussion. Let copies of Award be communicated to the Ministry for information and 
Notification. 


ANANDA KUMAR MUKHERJEE, Presiding Officer

and their workmen, received by the Central Government on 01/01/2026.

AWARD 


In exercise of powers conferred under clause (d) of Sub-section (1) and Sub-section (2A) of Section 10 of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Government of India through the Ministry of Labour, vide its Order L- 
22012/11/2020-IR(CM-ID) dated 09.06.2020 has been pleased to refer the following dispute between the employer, 
that is the Management of Kumardihi ‘B’ Colliery under Bankola Area of Eastern Coalfields Limited and their 
workman for adjudication by this Tribunal. 


“ Whether the action of the management of Kumardihi-B Colliery, Bankola Area, M/s. E.C.Ltd. in not 
providing employment on compassionate ground to Sri Rajesh Kumar Singh, S/O Late Binod Kumar Singh, 
Ex-employee of Kumardihi-B Colliery is justified? If not, what relief the dependent son of deceased workman 
is entitled to ? ” 


1 On receiving Order No. L-22025/01/2022-IR(CM-I) dated 11.09.2023 from the Government of India 
Ministry of Labour, New Delhi enclosing copy of Order No. L-22012/11/2020-IR(CM-II) dated 09.06.2020 a 
Reference case was registered on 25.09.2023. It appears that on earlier occasion the Order of Reference issued by the 
Government of India dated 09.06.2020 was not received by this office, hence this delay. Notice was issued to the 
parties through registered post, directing them to appear and submit their written statements along with relevant 
documents in support of their claims. 


7828 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JANUARY 10, 2026/PAUSHA 20, 1947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


2s Union filed written statement on 01.11.2023. Fact of the case which unfolds from in the written 
statement is that Binod Kumar Singh was a permanent employee of Eastern Coalfields Limited (hereinafter referred to 
as ECL). He was posted as a Driver at Kumardihi ‘B’ Colliery under Bahula Area of ECL and died in harness on 
18.10.2016 leaving behind Rajesh Kumar Singh as his dependent son and Binda Kora as his widow. Rajesh Kumar 
Singh informed the management about death of his father and prayed for his employment as per provisions of 
National Coal Wage Agreement (hereinafter referred to as NCWA). The management held medical examination of 
the dependent son at Central Hospital, Kalla on 03.05.2017 and report was submitted in Form ‘O’. The dependent 
submitted his Transfer Certificate issued by the School, Aadhaar Card, PAN Card, Voter ID Card, Ration Card where 
his date of birth was recorded as 04.05.1983. According to Service Record Excerpt of his father his age was ten years 
on 11.03.1991. The contention of the union is that Rajesh Kumar Singh was thirty-three years five months and 
fourteen days on the date of death of his father and being below thirty-five years of age on the date of death of his 
father he is entitled to employment under ECL as per provisions of Clause 9.3.4 of NCWA union prayed for directing 
the management to provide employment to the dependent son, being the only son of the deceased employee and his 
mother having expired earlier. 


3. Management contested the case by filing their written statement on 04.12.2023. According to the 
management Rajesh Kumar Singh submitted an application for employment under the provision of NCWA. Due to 
absence of authentic documents in support of his age Rajesh Kumar Singh was referred to the Medical Board for his 
medical examination at Central Hospital, Kalla. On 03.05.2017, after examination a report was submitted where the 
age of Rajesh Kumar Singh was assessed between 35 to 40 years. According to the management the mid-point of age 
is considered as age of the applicant on the date. Accordingly, the age of Rajesh Kumar Singh is calculated as thirty- 
seven years and six months on 03.05.2017 and he was found to be above the age of thirty-five years on the date of 
death of his father and therefore he is not eligible for employment as per provisions of 9.3.4 of NCWA. 


4. Union examined Rajesh Kumar Singh as Workman Witness No. 1. The witness field his affidavit-in-chief 
where he stated that his father died on 18.10.2016 while he was in the service of the company. As a dependent he 
filed an application for providing employment to him as per provisions of NCWA. After proper screening at the 
Colliery and Area Level he appeared for his Initial Medical Examination (hereinafter referred to as IME) before the 
IME Board at Central Hospital, Kalla. After medical examination the assessed age was not disclosed to him and the 
proposal for his employment was not processed on the ground that he was over-age. It is further stated that the 
management served a copy of letter addressed to the Personnel Manager (IC) where it was communicated to him that 
Rajesh Kumar Singh was over-age and he was not entitled to employment. The union stated that according to various 
documents his date of birth appeared as 04.05.1983. As per Service Records of his father management recorded the 
age of Rajesh Kumar Singh as ten years on 11.03.1991. Even his Birth Certificate disclosed that his date of birth is 
04.05.1983. According to the workman witness his age was 33 years 5 months and 14 days on the date of death of his 
father and being below 35 years, he stood eligible for employment. According to the witness IME Board deliberately 
decided his age above 35 years on the date of assessment without considering the Service Record and other 
documents. The witness asserted that his date of birth appearing in the Transfer Certificate issued by his School and 
Birth Certificate as well as the age recorded in the Service Record of his father should be accepted for providing 
employment to him. The workman witness produced the following documents in support of his case : 


(i) Copy of the Death Registration Certificate of Binod Kumar Singh is produced as Exhibit W-1. 


(ii) Copy of application of Rajesh Kumar Singh addressed to the Agent, Kumardihi ‘B’ Colliery, 
informing his father’s death, as Exhibit W-2. 


(iii) Copy of the Service Book of Binod Kumar Singh, as Exhibit W-3. 


(iv) Copy of the letter of Assistant Manager (Personnel), Kumardihi ‘B’ Colliery addressed to the Chief 
Manager (Personnel), Bankola Area, for IME of Rajesh Kumar Singh, as Exhibit W-4. 


(v) Copy of the No objection certificate in respect of Rajesh Kumar Singh, as Exhibit W-5. 
(vi) Copy of the Birth Registration Certificate of Rajesh Kumar Singh, as Exhibit W-6. 
(vii) Copy of the Aadhaar Card of Rajesh Kumar Singh, as Exhibit W-7. 


(Vili) Copy of the letter of the Sr. Manager (Personnel) Empl/ED addressed to the Sr. Manager 
(Personnel)/IC, Bankola Area for re-assessment of age of Rajesh Kumar Singh, as Exhibit W-8. 


(ix) Copy of the Transfer Certificate of Rajesh Kumar Singh issued by Fuleshwari Devi Memorial High 
School, as Exhibit W-9. 


3: In cross-examination the witness admitted his signature on Exhibit M-1, a letter dated 05/11.08.2017 issued 
by the Sr. Manager (Personnel)/IC, Bankola Area addressed to the Agent, Kumardihi ‘B’ Colliery wherein it was 
communicated that the Apex Medical Board assessed the age of Rajesh Kumar Singh on the date of death of Binod 
Kumar Singh as 36 years 11 months and 15 days, as such he is not eligible for employment as per NCWA. The age 
assessment report of Rajesh Kumar Singh has been marked as Exhibit M-2. It appears from the said document that his 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जनवरी 10, 2026/पौष 20, 1947 7829 


age was assessed as 35 to 40 years as on 03.05.2017 by way of holding X-Ray of the Lateral Spinal Cord, Mandible, 
Laryngeal Cartilage and Skull. Witness denied the suggestion that his claim for employment is not maintainable. 


6. Management examined Mr. Anuj Lakra, Manager (Personnel), Nakrakonda — Kumardihi ‘B’ Collieries as 
Management Witness No. 1. The witness in his affidavit-in-chief has reiterated the case disclosed by the management 
in their written statement. According to the witness the age of Rajesh Kumar Singh on 03.05.2017 was assessed 
between 35 to 40 years and it was communicated to him that he was disqualified for his employment due to over-age. 
In his evidence-in-chief Management Witness No. | deposed that Rajesh Kumar Singh exceeded the age for 
employment and for such reason his prayer for employment was regretted. The witness further deposed that Rajesh 
Kumar Singh was 36 years 11 months and 15 days of age at the time of death of his father and was not eligible for 
employment. 


7. In cross-examination the witness deposed that the age of Rajesh Kumar Singh was assessed in proper 
manner. The witness denied that the documents produced by Rajesh Kumar Singh showed that his age was below 35 
years on the date of death of his father. 


8. The point of contention in this case is whether the management of Kumardihi ‘B’ Colliery, Bahula Area, 
ECL is justified in not providing employment to Rajesh Kumar Singh on compassionate ground and what relief the 
dependent son of the deceased is entitled to. 


9. Mr. Rakesh Kumar, union representative advancing his argument submitted that according to the provisions 
of Clause 9.3.2 of NCWA the dependent son of the deceased employee is entitled to compassionate appointment on 
the death of his father. Clause 9.3.4 prescribes that for grant of compassionate appointment the concerned dependent 
should be physically fit and suitable for employment with age not more than 35 years. It is submitted that the 
dependent son by his letter dated 21.10.2016 informed the Agent / Manager, Kumardihi ‘B’ Colliery about the death 
of his father on 18.10.2016 and prayed for employment.A copy of letter is produced as Exhibit W-2. The dependent 
son was asked to appear before the screening committee of the management and thereafter his medical examination 
was held on 03.05.2017 where his age was assessed as 35 to 40 years. It is argued that as per Service Record the age 
of the dependent son was 33 years and 6 months on the date of death of his father. Mr. Rakesh Kumar heavily relied 
upon the Birth Certificate and School Leaving Certificate of Rajesh Kumar Singh which have been produced as 
Exhibit W-6 and W-9 respectively, where the date of birth is recorded as 04.05.1983. It is submitted that the deceased 
employee left behind Sarita Devi, a married daughter who had submitted her no objection in favour of his brother and 
the same is produced as Exhibit W-5. The union representative strenuously argued that when the School Leaving 
Certificate and Birth Registration Certificate were produced the management should have considered the same for 
determination of age and management should consider the guidelines laid down in Annexure-I of Implementation 
Instruction No. 76 of NCWA, relating to procedure for determination and verification of age. It is submitted that in 
case of non-matriculate but educated candidates where the candidate pursued studies in a recognized educational 
institution, the date of birth recorded in the School Leaving Certificate should be treated as correct date of birth and 
the same will not be altered under any circumstances. It is argued that in the instant case Rajesh Kumar Singh studied 
up to Class-IX at Fuleshwari Devi Memorial High School at Ukhra, Burdwan and is a literate person. Therefore, 
management should consider his date of birth as 04.05.1983 appearing in the Transfer Certificate / Schlool Leaving 
Certificate for the purpose of his employment. It is argued that the dependent son is entitled to employment. 


10. Mr. P. K. Goswami, learned advocate for the management of ECL argued that according to the Service Book 
of the deceased employee (Exhibit W-3), the age of Rajesh Kumar Singh was recorded as 10 years as on 11.03.1991. 
Therefore, his age may be computed as 35 years and 7 months on the date of death of his father on 18.10.2016. 
Learned advocate referred to the IME report (Exhibit M-2), where after holding X-ray examination of various parts of 
body the IME Board assessed the age of Rajesh Kumar Singh as 35 to 40 years on the date of examination 1.6., 
03.05.2017. It is argued that as per rules the mid-point of the assessed age is considered to be the age of the person on 
the date of examination and it is abundantly clear that the dependent son of the deceased employee is more than 35 
years of age on the date of death of his father and he was not eligible for employment as per Clause 9.3.4 of NCWA. 
Learned advocate argued that the Birth Certificate (Exhibit W-6) and Transfer Certificate (Exhibit W-9) are not 
reliable documents as the Birth Certificate was obtained in the year 2014 and it is not a cotemporaneous document. 
The union has not examined the Headmaster of the School to establish the date of birth appearing in the Transfer 
Certificate based upon the date appearing in the Admission Register. It is further argued that Annexure-I of the 
Implementation Instruction No. 76 of NCWA is applicable to the employees of the company for determining any 
dispute regarding their age and the same terms do not apply to the person claiming employment for the first time. 
Learned advocate submitted that the candidate claiming employment was informed by the management by letter dated 
05/11.08.2017 (Exhibit M-1) and 09.07.2018 (Exhibit W-8) that the age of Rajesh Kumar Singh was assessed by 
Central Hospital, Kalla and his proposal for employment is regretted for being over-age. It is argued that the 
dependent is not entitled for any employment. 


11. Perused the pleadings of the parties, evidence adduced and rival arguments advanced on behalf of the union 
and management. The bone of contention is whether the dependent son had already attained the age of 35 years on the 
date of death of his father, on 18.10.2016 and he is entitled to compassionate appointment under Clause 9.3.4 of 


7830 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JANUARY 10, 2026/PAUSHA 20, 1947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


NCWA. It is a well settled law that NCWA is a settlement under Section 2(p) of Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 and 
is binding upon the employer and employees under Section 18(3) of Industrial Disputes Act, 1947. The binding effect 
of NCWA has been discussed and clearly spelt out by the Hon'ble High Court at Calcutta in the cases of Eastern 
Coalfields Limited Vs. Kumari Kiran Singh and Others [AIRONLINE 2018 CAL 1593] and Sukumoni 
Hembram alias Sukumoni Mejhan Vs. Union of India and Others [MAT 27 of 2024]. Since the terms and 
conditions for granting employment to the dependent has been specified in the provisions of NCWA, any deviating 
from such terms is permissible for the purpose of granting employment to a person claiming for employment as a 
dependent. In the instant case initiating the process for employment of Rajesh Kumar Singh, management held age 
assessment through the Medical Board on 03.05.2017. The concerned dependent did no raise any objection against 
holding of age assessment examination by the Medical Board of the employer company. It appears from Exhibit M-2 
that scientific investigation was carried out for the purpose of assessing the age according to medical jurisprudence 
and his age was assessed between 35 to 40 years. The accepted position is that the midpoint of the range of age is 
accepted to be the age of a person for all functional purpose. Accordingly, it transpires that Rajesh Kumar Singh was 
36 years and 11 months on the date of death. From the Service Book of the deceased employee (Exhibit W-3) it is 
gathered that the age of Rajesh Kumar Singh was recorded as 10 years as on 11.03.1991, therefore his age on the date 
of death of his father can be assessed as 35 years, 7 months and 7 days and he is therefore found over-age for his 
employment. The union has produced two other relevant documents in support of the age of the claimant. A Birth 
Registration Certificate issued by Munger District Gram Panchayet Office, Government of Bihar dated 11.08.2014 
where the registration appears to have been made on 09.08.2014. The date of birth recorded in the certificate as 
04.05.1983 cannot be considered as sacrosanct as the registration has been made after a lapse of thirty-one years and 
the contents of such certificate which is not contemporaneous is only based upon hearsay material. The Transfer 
Certificate which is heavily relied upon by the union has been admitted in the evidence as Exhibit W-9. The document 
reveals that at the time of leaving the school on 30.04.1997 he was studying in Class-VIII and was promoted to Class- 
IX. The Headmaster of the School was not summoned by the union for proving the contents of the Transfer 
Certificate. The Admission Register was not produced for verification of such certificate. Under such circumstances I 
am not inclined to place reliance upon the Transfer Certificate for the purpose of accepting 04.05.1983 as the date of 
birth of Rajesh Kumar Singh. 


12. The union representative argued that Clause A (ii) of Annexure-I of the Implementation Instruction No. 76 of 
NCWA has to be followed for determining the age of non-matriculate but literate candidate by accepting the date of 
birth of the candidate appearing in the School Leaving Certificate. On a careful consideration I find that 
Implementation Instruction No. 76 is a part of NCWA and provides procedure for resolving age dispute of the 
employees and it also deals with various situation where the appointee is a matriculate, non-matriculate but educated, 
ex-serviceman, or an illiterate person. In my considered view such provisions are not applicable to persons who claim 
compassionate appointment. A claimant in order to succeed must strictly prove that he has fulfilled all the required 
terms and conditions for compassionate employment. In the present case the union has failed to prove that the 
dependent son was below 35 years of age on the date of death of his father. Therefore, I hold that Rajesh Kumar 
Singh is not entitled to compassionate employment under Clause 9.3.4 of NCWA. The management’s case is fortified 
by the report of IME as well as Service Book which prove that the claimant was over-age on 18.10.2016. The 
decision of the management of Kumardihi ‘B’ Colliery, Bankola Area, ECL in not providing employment to Rajesh 
Kumar Singh on compassionate ground is consistent with the facts and circumstances of the case and the provisions 
of NCWA binding the parties. The dependent son is consequently not entitled to any relief in this case. The Industrial 
Dispute is liable to be dismissed on contest. 


Hence, 
ORDERED 


that the Industrial Dispute is dismissed on contest. Rajesh Kumar Singh, dependent son of the deceased 
employee, Binod Kumar Singh is not entitled to any relief and I find no illegality in the action of management of 
Eastern Coalfields Limited in not providing employment to the son. Let an award be drawn up in light of my above 
findings. Let copies of the Award in duplicate be sent to the Ministry of Labour, Government of India, New Delhi for 
information and Notification. 


ANANDA KUMAR MUKHERJEE, Presiding Officer 


नई दिल्‍ली, 2 जनवरी, 2026

सरकार एसईसीएलके प्रबंधतंत्र के संबद्ध नियोजकों और उनके कर्मकारों के बीच, अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट औद्योगिक विवाद में 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जनवरी 10, 2026/पौष 20, 1947 7831

The Sub Area Manager, 
SECL, Rajgamar Sub Area,

Management 
(JUDGMENT) 


(Passed on this 17" day of October - 2025) 


As per letter dated 10/01/2019 by the Government of India, Ministry of Labour, New Delhi, the reference is 
made to this Tribunal under Section-10 of Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (in short the ‘Act’)as per Notification No. L- 
22012/166/2018 (IR(CM-II)) dt. 10/01/2019. The dispute under reference relates to: 


“WHETHER THE ACTION ON THE PART OF SUB AREA MANAGER SECL, RAJGAMAR 
COLLIERY IN NOT MAKING O.T PAYMENT ON THE BASIS OF O.T SLIP PEREORMED BY SHRI 
D.P. SARAF, EX- CHIEF STORE KEEPER AND JOINT SECRETARY OF RASTRIYA COLLIERY 
MAZDOOR CONGRESS AS PER THE ACTUAL ०.7 HOURS PERFORMED DURING THE PERIOD 
FROM 13-05-2013 TO 30-07-2017 IS APPROPRIATE AND JUSTIFIED? IF NOT, WHAT RELIEF 
SHRI D.P.SARAF, EX- CHIEF STORE KEEPER AND JOINT GENERAL SECRETARY OF RCMC IS 
ENTITLED TO?” 


Notices were issued to both the parties. They appeared and filed their respective statement of claim and 
defense. 


Case of the Workman is mainly that, he has been appointed as Chief Storekeeper with the Management 
from 13.05.2013 to 30.06.2017 and has superannuated on 30.06.2017 after completing the age for superannuation. 
While in his posting he was instructed by Management to collect different articles from Regional Store Korba Area, 


7832 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JANUARY 10, 2026/PAUSHA 20, 1947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


Central Warehouse Korba and Central Store which were about 17 to 18 kilometers from the place of his posting in 
Rajgamar. He was not provided any vehicle for this purpose. As per rules he is required to work only for six and half 
hours a day and is entitled to overtime for any hour in excess of this six and half hours period. He had to work for 
more than six and half hours prescribed for execution of the directions of Management to fetch the articles from these 
stores, and deposit them with Rajgamar store. He used to mention the time when he proceed to these stores and 
returned back to Rajgamar as well remained in the Rajgamar Office, which has been destroyed by Management. He 
had prepared worksheet mentioning the hours he worked, which was not counter signed by the Office of Management 
and when he claimed over time dues for the work he did in cases of his prescribed working hours, his this demand 
was also refused by the Management which is unjust, illegal and arbitrary. Thus, he has claimed that Management be 
directed to pay him the amount calculated by him as overtime with interest and fine. 


Case of Management is that, the Management used to send the Workman from Rajgamar Colliery to 
different stores mentioned by him for collecting articles for which he produced his TA bills and his expenses were 
reimbursed on the basis of his TA bills. It is further the case of the Management that, when a person is sent to another 
place for official job, he is required to be paid his travelling expenses and DA for the job date. There is no provision 
of payment of overtime allowances in such cases. Management has thus prayed that reference be answered against the 
Workman. 


Both the sides have filed affidavits and documents, which are different orders of Management directing him 
to visit the stores and collect the articles, TA Bills of the Workman. 


I have heard the Workman in person and Learned Senior Counsel Mr. Anoop Nair for the Management 
assisted by Learned Counsel Mr. Neeraj Kewat. I have gone through the record as well. 


The main argument from the side of Management is that, there is no provision for payment of overtime to a 
workman who is sent to a place different than the place where he is posted. In such cases, as per Rules the Workman 
is reimbursed his travelling expenses and TA for the date subject to Rules. 


Coal India Travelling Allowances Rules, 2010 
Para 10.2.2 & 10.2.5 requires to be referred in this respect and is being reproduced as follows: 
10.2.2 When the distance between the two places is 32 kilometers or less :- 


The amount reimbursable shall be the actual fare, by stage carriage (e.g. a bus) for non- 
executive cadre employees drawing pay less than Rs. 23479.55/- per month and in cases of other non- 
executive & executive cadre employees including Management Trainees, the actual fare provided that the 
journey is performed by taxi, auto-rickshaws, deluxe buses, AC buses etc. 


Note: Non-executive cadre employees drawing pay Rs.22149.01/- per month and above will be 
entitled to travel by the upper class in a stage carriage where more than one class is provided. They will be 
entitled to travel by deluxe buses also. (Authority: No. CIL/C-SA(PC)/ TA-2010/164 Date: 03.10.2012) 

Perusal of these Rules, makes it clear that there is no provision of payment of overtime in such 
situation. 


10.2.5 Reimbursement of local traveling expenses (RLTE) to employees for attending duties on 
Sundays/Holidays or during odd hours on working days: 


Reimbursement at the rate specified in Rule 10.2.1/10.2.2 from residence to place of duty and 
back, will be allowed subject to following conditions: 


a) Nosuch reimbursement will be allowed if Over Time Allowance has been paid or compensatory leave 
is allowed for duties performed. 


b) Odd hours for this purpose will be reckoned from 8-00 p.m. to 8-00 a.m. 


c) Where the journey by train is involved, reimbursement of Taxi/Auto fare or stage carriage charges 
will be limited to connecting distances between place of duty/place of residence and Railway station 
besides the Railway fare as per entitled class. 


(d) No employees will be entitled for reimbursement of local travelling expenses (RLTE) for attending 
duties on Sundays, Holidays or during odd hours on working days from residence to office and back. 
However, Executives will be reimbursed RLTE if they are on official duty from office to any other 
place. 


Hence, in the light of above discussion, holding the action of Management in not granting the 
Workman overtime for the extra hours on work since he was paid travelling allowances when he was sent to 
some other places for collection of materials by Management is held just, legal and proper and the reference 
stands answered accordingly. 


DATE:- 17/10/2025 
P.K. SRIVASTAVA, Presiding Officer 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(1)] भारत का राजपत्र : जनवरी 10, 2026/पौष 20, 1947 7833 


नई दिल्‍ली, 2 जनवरी, 2026 
का.आ. 2305.---औद्योगिक विवाद अधिनियम, 1947 (1947 का 14) की धारा 17 के अनुसरण में, केन्द्रीय 
सरकार एसईसीएलके प्रबंधतंत्र के संबद्ध नियोजकों और उनके कर्मकारों के बीच, अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट औद्योगिक विवाद में 
केन्द्रीय सरकार औद्योगिक अधिकरण-सह-श्रम न्यायालय, जबलपुर के Gare (एलसी/आर-126/2012) को प्रकाशित 
करती है, जो केन्द्रीय सरकार को 01/01/2026 को प्राप्त हुआ था। 


[सं. एल-22012/136/2012-आईआर (सी.एम-1)] 
सलोनी, उप निदेशक/लिंक अधिकारी

Government hereby publishes the Award (Reference. LC/R/126/2012) of the Central Government Industrial 
Tribunal-cum-Labour Court, Jabalpur as shown in the Annexure, in the industrial dispute between the Management 
of SECL and their workmen, received by the Central Government on 01/01/2026. 


[No. L-22012/136/2012— IR (CM-ID]

Workman 
Versues 

The General Manager, 

SECL Kusmunda Area, 

Post- Kusmunda, 

District — Korba (C.G.) 

Management 
(JUDGMENT) 


(Passed on this 13 “day of November - 2025) 


As per letter dated 05/11/2012 of the Government of India, Ministry of Labour, New Delhi, the reference is 
made to this Tribunal under Section-10 of Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (in short the ‘Act’)as per Notification No. L- 
22012/136/2012 (IR(CM-I)) dt. 05/11/2012. The dispute under reference relates to: 


“Whether the action of the management of General Manager, Kusmunda Area of SECL, District Korba 
(CG) not given the notional seniority in EP Fitter cum Mechanic Grade-I, in Category-'B' to Shri S.R. 
Maneshwar of Kusmunda Area as per the Arbitration award with effect from 23.12.1988 at par as given to 
their after workmen in SECL Gevra Area was legal and justified? What relief the said workman is entitled 
to and from what date?” 


After registering the case on the basis of reference Notices were issued to both the parties. They appeared 
and filed their respective statement of claim and defense. 


7834 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JANUARY 10, 2026/PAUSHA 20, 1947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


The undisputed facts are that, the Applicant Workman Shesh Ram Maneshwar was appointed in the Korba 
West Area of SECL as Mechanic (trainee) along with three other workers namely Dhani Ram Patle, L.P. Rahangdale 
and Lakhan Lal Jhothe vide appointment letter dated SECL/GM/KB(W)/PM/APPMNT/87/3977 dated 12.11.1987. 
Later on, the Korba West Area was split into three different areas viz; Gevra, Kusmunda and Korba. The three 
Workmen i.e. Dhani Ram Patle, L.P. Rahangdale and Lakhan Lal Jhothe were posted in Gevra Area whereas the 
Applicant Workman was posted in Kusmunda Area. Dhani Ram Patle claimed notional seniority before the 
Management and his case was sent for joint arbitration. Award dated 23.12.1992 was passed by the Arbitrators 
holding the Workman Dhani Ram Patle entitled to be regularized as Fitter cum Mechanic Grade-I in Group — B on 
completion of his successful one year training. The remaining two workers i.e. L.P. Rahangdale and Lakhan Lal 
Jhothe also claimed the same regularization on the basis of Arbitration Award and they were promoted notionally in 
seniority in Grade — I Group - B wee.f. 23.12.1988 vide Office order No. SECL/DGM/GBR/95/4021. The 
Management refused same benefit to the Applicant Workman on the ground that he was posted in Kusmunda Area of 
the Company and not in the Gevra Area where other workmen, who were benefited, were posted. The Management 
further took the ground that, the Award was with respect to Workman D.R. Patle only posted in Gevra Area and 
because it will disturb the settled seniority list in the Kusmunda where the applicant workman is posted. Another case 
of Management is that, the claim is barre,d by unexplained delay and latches. 


The Workman side has filed Office Order dated 10.11.1995/12.12.1995 regarding promotion of L.P. 
Rahangdale and Lakhan Lal Jhothe w.e.f. 23.12.1986 with D.R. Patle as per arbitration Award, Copy of petition 
before Labour Commissioner, reply of Management, Rejoinder of Union, Failure report which are Exhibit W-1 to W- 
6. Management has filed the memorandum of settlement with regard to promotion of L.P. Rahangdale and Lakhan Lal 
Jhothe in the light of Award with respect to D.R. Patle. An Office Order dated 10.11.1995/12.12.1995 which have 
been filed by the Workman Union also. 


Both the sides have filed affidavit with respect to their claims and defense. It also appears that, the 
Workman died during the pendency of the Case but since the case is espoused by the Union and Act specifically 
provides that no reference was abated on the basis of Claimant Workman. This reference will also not abate. 


None was present for Workman Union at the time of argument. I have heard argument of Learned Counsel 
Mr. Neeraj Kewat for the Management and I have gone through the record. 


From perusal of record, it establishes that four persons including the claimant workman were appointed on 
one day in Korba West Area and worked together in the same area, later on this Korba Area was divided into three 
areas. Three employees went to Gevra Area and the claimant Workman was absorbed in the Kusmunda Area. Three 
co-workers of the Claimant Workman with identical claims were granted their claims by the Management were else 
the claimant workman was refused the relief on the ground that he was in a different area. It is not disputed all these 
areas are controlled and managed as well governed by the one Company South Eastern Coal Field Limited which is 
itself a subsidiary of Coal India. In these circumstances, refusal of relief of promotion as claimed by the Claimant 
Workman in parity with other beneficiaries, who had identical claims, only on the ground that it will disturb the 
settled seniority list in the area is nothing but arbitrary and is in violation of Article 14 of the Constitution of India. 


Hence, in the light of above discussion and findings the Claimant Workman Shesh Ram Maneshwar is 
also held entitled to be promoted from Fitter cum Mechanic Grade-I Category-C to Category-B after 
completion of his successful training of one year i.e. from 23.12.1988 in parity with the Workman Dhani Ram 
Patle, L.P. Rahangdale and Lakhan Lal Jhothe and is held entitled to get all benefits in this respect from 
Management accordingly treating him to be notionally promoted from 23.12.1988. However, the mandatory 
benefits could be given from 12.12.1995 i.e. the date of promotion of other co-workers mentioned above. 


The reference stands answered accordingly. 
AWARD 


Holding the action of the management of General Manager, Kusmunda Area of SECL, District Korba 
(CG) not given the notional seniority in EP Fitter cum Mechanic Grade-I, in Category-'B' to Shri S.R. Maneshwar 
of Kusmunda Area as per the Arbitration award with effect from 23.12.1988 at par as given to their after workmen 
in SECL Gevra Area illegal, arbitrary and unjustified, he is held entitled to get all benefits in this respect from 
Management accordingly treating him to be notionally promoted from 23.12.1988. However, the mandatory 
benefits could be given from 12.12.1995 i.e. the date of promotion of other co-workers mentioned above. 


No order as to cost. 
DATE:- 13/11/2025 
P.K. SRIVASTAVA, Presiding Officer 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जनवरी 10, 2026/पौष 20, 1947 7835

सरकार एन सी एल के प्रबंधतंत्र के संबद्ध नियोजकों और उनके कर्मकारों के बीच, अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट औद्योगिक विवाद में

(JUDGMENT) 
(Passed on this 107 day of November, 2025) 


As per letter dated 25/07/2012 by the Government of India, Ministry of Labour, New Delhi, the reference 
has been made to this Tribunal under Section-10 of Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (in short the ‘Act’) as per 
Notification No. L-22012/86/2012(IR(CM-IT)) dt. 25/07/2012. The dispute under reference relates to:- 


“Whether the action of the General Manager, Central Workshop, Jayant Project of NCL, in imposing 
punishment of Suspension, Stoppage of increment & Penal rent, on an alleged misconduct of illegal 
occupying of the quarter by Shri J.P. Singh, Fitter (vacated during the course of enquiry proceedings), is 
legal and justified? To what relief the claimant is entitled for, and from which date?” 


7836 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JANUARY 10, 2026/PAUSHA 20, 1947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


After registering the case on the basis of reference, notices were issued to the parties. They appeared and 
filed their respective statement of claim in defense. 


The case of the workman is mainly that while he was working as Fitter with the management, he was 
issued a charge sheet alleging misconduct under clause 26.14 of certified standing orders on his part by way of 
illegally and unauthorizedly breaking open residential accommodation MQ-463 of management and occupying it 
illegally without any allotment in his favour. This charge sheet dated 11.09.2000 was replied by him but ignoring his 
reply, the management conducted an enquiry with respect to the allegations arbitrarily and without observing 
principles of natural justice. Charges were wrongly held proved and the punishment in form of stoppage of one 
increment with cumulative effect as well penal rent imposed on the workman is also excessive to the misconduct. 


The workman has prayed that holding this action of management against law and unjustified, the punishment 
be set aside. 


Case of the management is that the workman has been in habit of committing misconduct, he was issued a 
charge sheet on 12.07.1995 alleging misconduct as defined under clause 26.3, 26.5, 26.16 and 26.22 of certified 
standing orders, which were enquired into and after the charges were proved in enquiry he was punished. He did not 
improve himself and repeated the misconduct in the year 2000 by way of unauthorizedly and illegally breaking open 
the company’s accommodation No. 459-Q forcibly and taking illegal possession in the said residence which is 
misconduct under clause 26.14 of certified standing orders, after his reply was found in sufficient, an enquiry was 
conducted, as per rules the workman was found guilty of misconduct in the enquiry and he was punished, which is not 
excessive to the misconduct proved and keeping in view the fact that he is a habitual offender; management has 
requested that the reference be answered against the workman. 


Following Preliminary Issue was framed on the basis of pleadings:- 
Whether the departmental enquiry conducted is legal and proper? 


On the basis of evidence, this issue was answered vide order dated 23.02.2024, the departmental enquiry 
was held vitiated in law, management was granted opportunity to prove the charge before this Tribunal. This order is 
part of this judgment and award. 


The management filed affidavit of its witness Anand Murti, General Manager in support of charge as his 
Examination-in-chief. He was cross-examined by workman side, he has proved the documents which were proved 
during the hearing of preliminary issue. 


No evidence in rebuttal was filed by workman. 


I have heard argument of Learned Counsel for workman Mr. Arun Patel and Mr. Neeraj Kewat, Learned 
Counsel for the management. I have gone through the record as well. 


On perusal of record in light of rival arguments, following Issues came up for determination:- 


4) Whether the charges are proved from the evidence before this Tribunal? 
2) Whether the punishment is proportionate to the charges proved? 
Issue No.1, 


Charges have been elaborated earlier, the management witness has stated that the workman entered in 
quarter No. MQ-463 illegally after breaking lock, inspite of the fact that he was allotted quarter No. MQ-458 and 
occupied the said quarter No. MQ-463 illegally. He referred to document M-E/9 entry dated 16.03.1989 in this 
respect which is handing over or taking over register. This witness also states that later on the workman vacated the 
MQ-463 which he had illegally occupied after breaking open its lock. An enquiry was conducted in this respect and 
was punished with permanent stoppage of one increment and deduction of penal rent for illegally occupying for the 
period he had illegally occupied the accommodation. 


There is nothing in his cross-examination to discredit this witness. 


The case of the workman is that he was entitled to one accommodation from the company residence 
which was vacant, it has been the practice and it is usual for the employees who break open the vacant residence and 
enter. Thereafter, the company allots the residence to the workman. Learned counsel for workman has submitted that 
since the workman was entitled to one accommodation which was not being provided by the management inspite of 
the fact that there were vacant accommodations available and there were incidences of such nature earlier also, in 
which the workers broke open the vacant accommodation and took possession of the accommodations without 
allotment, the workman could not be held guilty of the misconduct. 


I am not inclined to accept this argument on the ground that if the workman was not allotted any 
accommodation, he would be getting house rent allowances and only entitlement does not gave anyone license to 
commit illegality. Thus, when there is clear evidence that the workman illegally occupied the accommodation without 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जनवरी 10, 2026/पौष 20, 1947 7837 


allotment, he cannot be exonerated from the charge only on the ground that he was entitled to get one accommodation 
and accommodations were vacant. Hence, in light of above discussion, the charge of misconduct is held proved 
against the workman. 


Issue No. 1 is answered accordingly. 
Issue No. 2, 


Workman has been awarded punishment of permanent stoppage of one increment and recovery of penal rent 
from him as well pay of suspension period has also not been given to him by way of punishment. Learned counsel for 
workman has submitted that this is an excessive punishment compared to the charges. His main grievance is with 
respect to permanent stoppage of one increment in the punishment order which is its replicating effect. 


On the other hand, it has been submitted by learned counsel for management that the workman is a habitual 
offender, he has committed misconduct earlier also for which he has been punished. Hence, keeping in view these 
facts the punishment order cannot be said to be an excessive one. He further submits that the punishment order does 
not warrant any interference. 


Settled proposition of law is that the punishment is domain of Executive and Courts and Tribunals, who be 
hesitant in interfering with the punishment orders until and unless they are satisfied, it is so disproportionate that it 
shocks the conscious of the Court or Tribunal. 


To consider whether the punishment order is shockingly disproportionate to the charge, the nature and effect 
of the misconduct proved, previous conduct will entirely be the factors to be considered. 


In the case in hand, undisputedly, the workman has a history of misconduct. Permanent stoppage of 
increment has its replicating effect and it harms the workman till his superannuation and also thereafter. The workman 
was entitled to accommodation from the management which were vacated at the time of misconduct but the process 
adopted by workman was not a correct way of enforcing his rights. This fact that after another accommodation was 
allotted to him, the workman vacated the said accommodation which he had occupied forcibly, the punishment of 
cumulative stoppage appears too harsh. Hence, keeping in this fact, the sentence of cumulative stoppage of one 
increment is converted into stoppage of one increment for five years and the punishment stands modified accordingly. 


Issue No. 2 stands answered accordingly. 
In light of above discussion and findings, the reference is answered as follows.- 
AWARD 


“Holding the action of the General Manager, Central Workshop, Jayant Project of NCL, in imposing 
punishment of Suspension, Stoppage of increment & Penal rent, on an alleged misconduct of illegal occupying of 
the quarter by Shri J.P. Singh, Fitter (vacated during the course of enquiry proceedings), excessive, the stoppage of 
one increment with cumulative effect is converted into stoppage of one increment for five years.” 


No order as to cost. 
DATE:- 10-11-2025 
P.K. SRIVASTAVA, Presiding Officer
